\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}^{2}\asymp\mathrm{diam}(K)^{2}\asymp n^{1/2}
. Since here
w(K)\asymp\sqrt{n}
and
\sigma\asymp 1
, we obtain an example where the bound
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\lesssim\sqrt{\sigma w(K)}
from Remark
2.8
is tight. One can also check that
\varepsilon^{\ast}\asymp 1
, which means the bound from Corollary
2.12
of
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\lesssim\sqrt{\sigma}\sqrt{\varepsilon^{*}}C_{n}\sqrt[4]{n}
is tight (without the logarithmic factors from
C_{n}
), as we mentioned in Remark
2.13
.

We provide another example which does not even require
d_{n-1}\gg d_{n}
. Consider the Sobolev type ellipsoid with
d_{k}=(n-k+1)^{\alpha}
for
0<\alpha<1/2
. Then we can calculate
\sigma\asymp\sqrt{n^{-1+2\alpha}}\ll 1\asymp\mathrm{diam}(K)
. The conditions
1/d_{n-k}^{2}\leq(k+1)\sigma^{2}
but
1/d_{n-k+1}^{2}>k\sigma^{2}
are equivalent to
(k+1)^{1+2\alpha}\geq\sigma^{-2}\geq k^{1+2\alpha}
, so we take
k=\lceil n^{(1-2\alpha)/(1+2\alpha)}\rceil
. Hence the minimax rate is

\displaystyle\lceil n^{(1-2\alpha)/(1+2\alpha)}\rceil n^{-1+2\alpha}

\displaystyle\asymp n^{-2\alpha(1-2\alpha)/(1+2\alpha)}

\displaystyle\ll 1\asymp\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)^{2}.

This result complements that of
Wei et al. (2020)
who consider the
\alpha>1/2
case by contrast and demonstrate optimality of the LSE.

3.2.6
\ell_{p}
balls for
p\in(1,2)

In order to show the suboptimality of the LSE for the
\ell_{p}
unit ball for a fixed
p\in(1,2)
, we first take a detour and prove a general bound on the LSE rate for a strongly convex body
K
. A body
K
is called strongly convex if there exists a constant
k
such that for any
\mu,\nu\in K
and any
\lambda\in[0,1]
we have that
B(\lambda\mu+(1-\lambda)\nu,k\lambda(1-\lambda)\|\mu-\nu\|^{2})\subset K
. Let
\mu
and
\nu
be opposite ends of a diameter of
K
and pick
\lambda=1/2
. Then, by strong convexity,
B((\mu+\nu)/2,kd^{2}/4)\subseteq K
so that
2kd^{2}/4<d
. Rearranging
k<2d^{-1}
.

Lemma 3.16
.

Let
K
be a strongly convex body with parameter
k
. Suppose
\sigma\asymp(k\sqrt{n})^{-1}
. Then
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\asymp d
.

Returning to
\ell_{p}
balls for
p\in(1,2)
, by
Garber and Hazan (2015, Corollary 1)
, we know that
\ell_{p}
unit balls are
k=(p-1)n^{1/2-1/p}
strongly convex. Hence for
\sigma\asymp 1/n^{1-1/p}
, we have
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\asymp d
from Lemma
3.16
. Now, it is easy to verify that the diameter of the
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
-balls are both 2, and since for any
p\in(1,2)
we have that the unit
\ell_{1}
ball is a subset of the unit
\ell_{p}
-ball which is a subset of the
\ell_{2}
-ball, it follows that
d\asymp 1
.

On the other hand, following Section III.E of
Neykov (2022)
(formally that section treats convex weak
\ell_{p}
balls but since the packing numbers are the same it applies to
\ell_{p}
balls as well) we know that the minimax rate satisfies
\varepsilon^{\ast}(\sigma)\asymp\sigma^{1-p/2}(\log n)^{(2-p)/4}\wedge d
for values of
\sigma
satisfying
\log(n\sigma^{p}(\log n)^{p/2})\asymp\log n
and
\sigma^{(4-2p)/4}(\log n)^{(2-p)/4}\gtrsim n^{1/2-1/p}
.

Observe that for
\sigma\asymp 1/n^{1-1/p}
we have
\sigma^{1-p/2}(\log n)^{(2-p)/4}\ll 1\asymp d
which implies
\varepsilon^{\ast}(\sigma)\ll 1
provided the other two conditions hold. To see this, first note that
\log n\ll n^{\alpha}
for any
\alpha>0
. Thus,

\displaystyle\sigma^{1-p/2}(\log n)^{(2-p)/4}

\displaystyle\asymp n^{1/p-3/2+p/2}(\log n)^{(2-p)/4}

\displaystyle\ll n^{1/p-3/2+p/2+\alpha/2-\alpha p/4}.

Hence if we take
0<\alpha<\frac{1/p-3/2+p/2}{p/4-1/2}=\frac{2(p-1)}{p}
, then the right-hand side is of the form
n^{\beta}
for
\beta<0
and is thus
\ll 1\asymp d
.

We must check that
\sigma\asymp 1/n^{1-1/p}
satisfies the other two conditions. First

\displaystyle\log(n\sigma^{p}(\log n)^{p/2})=\log(n^{2-p}(\log n)^{p/2})\asymp\log n.

Next, using the fact that
(\log n)^{(2-p)/4}\gtrsim 1
and substituting
\sigma\asymp 1/n^{1-1/p}
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma^{(4-2p)/4}(\log n)^{(2-p)/4}\gtrsim n^{1/p-3/2+p/2}\geq n^{1/2-1/p},

where the final inequality follows since
p/2+2/p\geq 2
for any
p>0
(using the AM-GM inequality). This proves that
\varepsilon^{\ast}(\sigma)\ll 1
while by the previous lemma,
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\asymp 1
. It follows that for
p\in(1,2)
, the LSE is suboptimal for
\ell_{p}
balls for
\sigma\asymp 1/n^{1-1/p}
.

4
Discussion

We have established numerous necessary or sufficient conditions for minimax optimality of the constrained least squares estimator in the convex Gaussian sequence model setting. Our techniques focused on the local behavior of the Gaussian width and metric entropy of the set. We then provided a series of examples where the LSE is minimax optimal or suboptimal for noise
\sigma
chosen in an appropriate range. Our examples included isotonic regression in both one and many dimensions, hyper-rectangles, ellipsoids, and
\ell_{p}
balls with
p\in[1,2]
. Our results also lead to theoretical algorithms (Appendix
A
) that bound the worst case LSE rate.

Future work could consider extensions to handle estimation with sub-Gaussian noise, which may clash with our use of the Gaussian width following
Chatterjee (2014)
. Moreover, our examples with
\ell_{p}
balls could be extended for the
p>2
case. While a formidable task, our examples of LSE-suboptimality underscore the need for a general algorithm to replace the LSE while remaining computationally tractable.

Appendix A
Algorithms searching for the worst case rate of the LSE on bounded sets

The results of Section
2.2
, in particular Theorems
2.14
and
2.15
, inspire theoretical algorithms for searching for worst case rate for the LSE for bounded sets
K
. Our Algorithm
2
below is based on local packings while Algorithm
3
instead uses global packings. Since both algorithms make usage of evaluating the map
\nu\mapsto w_{\nu}(\varepsilon)
, before we introduce them we will illustrate how one can evaluate the map
\nu\mapsto w_{\nu}(\varepsilon)
on a given closed convex body
K
, where it is assumed that we have a separation oracle for
K
. A separation oracle for
K
is a function
\mathcal{O}_{K}\colon\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
\mathcal{O}_{K}(\nu)=0
if
\nu\in K
and if
\nu\not\in K
,
\mathcal{O}_{K}(\nu)=a
where
a\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
is such that
a{{}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}\nu>a{{}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}\mu
for all points
\mu\in K
. The next lemma states that if one can compute the LSE for
K
, then assuming a separation oracle for
K
is not a stringent assumption.

Lemma A.1
.

If one can project on
K
, i.e., one can calculate
\Pi_{K}\nu
for any
\nu\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, then one has a separation oracle on
K
.

Proof.

Define
\mathcal{O}_{K}(\nu)=\nu-\Pi_{K}\nu
for all
\nu\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then
\mathcal{O}_{K}(\nu)=0
for all
\nu\in K
. If
\nu\not\in K
, observe that for any
\mu\in K
we have
(\nu-\Pi_{K}\nu){{}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}(\nu-\mu)>0
. This follows by
Bellec [2018, equation (1.19)]
since

\displaystyle(\nu-\Pi_{K}\nu)(\mu-\nu)+\|\nu-\Pi_{K}\nu\|^{2}

\displaystyle=(\nu-\Pi_{K}\nu){{}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}(\mu-\Pi_{K}(\nu))

\displaystyle\leq 0,

and also that
\|\nu-\Pi_{K}\nu\|>0
since
\nu\not\in K
.
∎

The idea for calculating the width
w_{\nu}(\varepsilon)
is simple, and we summarize it in Algorithm
1
. We will sample
N
points
\xi_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbb{I}_{n})
,
i\in[N]
. Assume for a moment we can calculate the values of
\sup_{\eta\in B(\nu,\varepsilon)\cap K}\langle\xi_{i},\eta-\nu\rangle
. We will then average all these values as an estimate of
w_{\nu}(\varepsilon)
. This strategy works because of the celebrated concentration inequality of Lipschitz functions of Gaussian variables
[e.g.,
Wainwright, 2019
, Theorem 2.26]
, noting that the map
x\mapsto\sup_{\eta\in B(\nu,\varepsilon)\cap K}\langle x,\eta-\nu\rangle
is
\varepsilon
-Lipschitz
[see
Wainwright, 2019
, Example 2.30]
. The concentration result states that
\langle\xi_{i},\eta^{*}_{i}-\nu\rangle
(where
\eta^{*}_{i}
denotes the maximum of
\sup_{\eta\in B(\nu,\varepsilon)\cap K}\langle\xi_{i},\eta-\nu\rangle
) is a sub-Gaussian random variable with variance proxy
\varepsilon^{2}
, hence satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\big|N^{-1}\sum_{i\in[N]}\langle\xi_{i},\eta^{*}_{i}-\nu\rangle-w_{\nu}(\varepsilon)\big|\geq t\bigg)\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{Nt^{2}}{2\varepsilon^{2}}\right).

Thus if one wants to approximate
w_{\nu}(\varepsilon)
to precision
t
with probability at least
1-\delta
, then one needs at most
N=2\varepsilon^{2}\log(2/\delta)/t^{2}
observations.

To solve
\sup_{\eta\in B(\nu,\varepsilon)\cap K}\langle\xi_{i},\eta-\nu\rangle
for a fixed
\xi
given a separation oracle for
K
, we use the ellipsoid algorithm
[
Grötschel et al., 2012
, Chapter 3]
. At each iteration, we must either find a separation oracle for
B(\nu,\varepsilon)
(which is trivial by Lemma
A.1
), or a separation oracle for
K
. If the point
\eta
happens to be in the set
B(\nu,\varepsilon)\cap K
then one simply needs to take the gradient of
-\langle\xi,\eta-\nu\rangle
with respect to
\eta
which is also trivial.

Algorithm 1

Local Gaussian Width Algorithm

Input:

K
a compact convex set in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, a separation oracle
\mathcal{O}_{K}
for
K
, a point
\nu\in K
,
\varepsilon>0
, desired precision
t
and probability
1-\delta
.

1

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a subroutine that can solve
\arg\sup_{\eta\in B(\nu,\varepsilon)\cap K}\langle\xi,\eta-\nu\rangle
given
\mathcal{O}_{K}
;

2

Set
N=\lceil 2\varepsilon^{2}\log(2/\delta)/t^{2}\rceil
;

3

Draw
\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{N}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbb{I}_{n})
;

4

Compute using
\eta_{i}^{\ast}=\arg\sup_{\eta\in B(\nu,\varepsilon)\cap K}\langle\xi_{i},\eta-\nu\rangle
for
i=1,\dots,N
using
\mathcal{E}
;

5

Set
\widehat{w_{\nu}}(\varepsilon)=N^{-1}\sum_{i\in[N]}\langle\xi_{i},\eta_{i}^{\ast}-\nu\rangle
;

6

return

\widehat{w_{\nu}}(\varepsilon)

A.1
A local packing algorithm

Algorithm 2

Local Packing Algorithm

Input:

Compact convex set
K
, diameter
d
, constant
c^{\ast}
from definition of local metric entropy

1

Function

ChildrenDistance(
\nu\in K
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
)
:

2

\delta\leftarrow\frac{d}{2^{k-1}c^{\ast}}
;

3

Form a maximal
\delta
-packing set
\{\nu_{1}^{k},\nu_{2}^{k},\dots,\nu_{M}^{k}\}
of the set
B(\nu,\delta c^{\ast})\cap K
where
M=\mathcal{M}(\delta,B(\nu,\delta c^{\ast})\cap K)
;

4

Solve
\nu_{i}^{k\ast}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{i}w_{\nu_{i}^{k}}(\delta)
;

5

\Psi\leftarrow w_{\nu}(\delta c^{\ast})-w_{\nu_{i}^{k\ast}}(\delta)
;

6

T\leftarrow C(\delta c^{\ast})^{2}/(2\sigma
);

7

return

\Psi-T
;

8

Initialize
\nu^{\ast}\in K
;

9

Set
\phi(\nu^{\ast})=1
;
/*
Track the level of any node
\nu
in the tree */

10

Let
Q=(\nu^{\ast})
be an ordered queue;

11

while

Q
is not empty

do

12

Remove the point
\nu
at the end of the
Q
;

13

Compute
\gamma=

ChildrenDistance(
\nu
,
\phi(\nu)
)
and the packing set therein;

14

Define
\beta=\dfrac{d}{2^{\phi(\nu)-1}c^{*}}
;

15

if

\gamma>0

then

16

return

\beta

17

else

18

For each child node
\nu_{i}
in the packing set, set
\phi(\nu_{i})=\phi(\nu)+1
;

19

Add each
\nu_{i}
to the start of
Q
;

Suppose
K
is a compact convex set with diameter
d
. Take
c^{\ast}>4
in the definition of the local metric entropy, and set
C=4-\frac{1}{{c^{\ast}}^{2}}
. We now consider partitioning
K
in the following way. Begin by fixing an arbitrary point
\nu^{*}\in K
(its location is inconsequential). Maximally pack the set
B(\nu^{*},d)\cap K=K
at a distance
d/c^{\ast}
. Next, for each point
\nu_{i}^{*}
in that packing set, consider maximally packing the set
B(\nu_{i}^{*},d/2)\cap K
at a distance
d/(2c^{\ast})
. Continue the process infinitely, obtaining an infinite tree of packing sets. At the
k
th level, the number of descendants of a point from the
(k-1)
th level is bounded by
\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(d/2^{k-1})
.

Following this packing set construction, we proceed with our algorithm starting with level
k=1
. Algorithm
2
considers the difference between
w(B(\nu^{*},d/2^{k-1})\cap K)
and the smallest possible point from the children of
\nu^{*}
, i.e.,
\min_{i}w(B(\nu_{i}^{*},d/(2^{k-1}c^{\ast}))\cap K)
. If that difference is bigger than
Cd^{2}/(2\sigma)
then the algorithm stops and outputs
d/c^{\ast}
. If not, it proceeds to look at all children of
\nu^{*}
in the same way but replacing
d
with
d/2
. For example, if we take
\nu_{i}^{*}
the algorithm looks at the difference between
w(B(\nu^{*}_{i},d/2^{2-1})\cap K)
and the smallest possible difference from its children. If the algorithm finds a point whose corresponding difference exceeds
C(d/2)^{2}/(2\sigma)
, the algorithm terminates and outputs
d/(2c^{\ast})
. Else, it continues to look at the children of children, and so on, in a breadth-first search manner.

The following theorem (which uses Lemma
E.1
in the Appendix) states our resulting bounds on
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)
depending on how many iterations it takes for the algorithm to terminate.

Theorem A.2
.

Define
c^{\prime}=\frac{(2c^{\ast}-4)(4c^{\ast}-1/c^{\ast})}{(c^{\ast}-4)c^{\ast}}
for some
c^{\ast}>4
.

(i)

Suppose Algorithm
2
terminates after
k
iterations. Then
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\gtrsim d/(2^{k-1}c^{\ast})
.

(ii)

Suppose Algorithm
2
does not terminate within
k
iterations.

(a)

If
\sigma\geq c^{\prime}\cdot\frac{d}{2^{k}}
, then
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\asymp\sigma\wedge d
.

(b)

If
\sigma\leq c^{\prime}\cdot\frac{d}{2^{k}}
, then
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\lesssim c^{\prime}\cdot\frac{d}{2^{k}}
.

A.2
A global packing algorithm

In this subsection, we present a global packing algorithm based on Theorem
2.15
. We use the same absolute constants
L,c^{\ast},
and
C
from the theorem. For a fixed
\sigma>0
, Algorithm
3
(if it does not immediately terminate) produces an
\varepsilon
that will either match
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}
or upper bound it. The algorithm accomplishes this by repeatedly forming
\delta
-packing sets of
K
with
\delta=\left(\varepsilon^{3}/(4c^{\ast}\sup_{\eta\in K}w_{\eta}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})\sigma)\right)\wedge\varepsilon
, and doubling
\varepsilon
until a certain condition is met.

Algorithm 3

Global Packing Algorithm

Input:

K
a convex set,
\sigma>0
, absolute constants
c^{\ast},L>0
, and
C=8+8/c^{\ast}
.

1

Initialize
\varepsilon\leftarrow 2\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
where
\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
is defined in (
2.3
);

2

\delta\leftarrow\varepsilon^{3}/(4c^{\ast}\sup_{\eta\in K}w_{\eta}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})\sigma)\wedge\varepsilon
;

3

Form a maximal
\delta
-packing set
\{\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\ldots,\nu_{M}\}
of
K
, where
M=\mathcal{M}(\delta,K)
;

4

\Psi\leftarrow\max_{i\in[M]}\sup_{\nu^{\prime}\in B(\nu_{i},2\varepsilon-\delta)\cap K}w_{\nu^{\prime}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-w_{\nu_{i}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})
;

5

T\leftarrow C\varepsilon^{2}/(2\sigma)-(L/c^{\ast})\cdot\varepsilon\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}_{K}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)}
;

6

while

\Psi\geq T

do

7

\varepsilon\leftarrow 2\varepsilon
;

8

Update
\delta
;

9

Update maximal
\delta
-packing set
\{\nu_{1},\dots,\nu_{M}\}
of
K
with
M=\mathcal{M}(\delta,K)
;

10

Update
\Psi
and
T
;

11

return

\varepsilon

In computing
\Psi
from the algorithm, we must solve the problem
\sup_{\nu^{\prime}\in K\cap B(\nu_{i},2\varepsilon-\delta)}w_{\nu^{\prime}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})
for each
i
. This is a maximization of a concave function over a convex set and in principle is computationally tractable.

Our main result is stated in Theorem
A.3
, which relates the output
\varepsilon
of Algorithm
3
to
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)
. The proof uses Lemma
E.3
to compare
\varepsilon
to
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)
, and that lemma in turn relies on Lemma
E.2
to bound
\varepsilon
depending on whether
\Psi<T
or
\Psi\geq T
(the algorithm’s stopping conditions).

Theorem A.3
.

Suppose Algorithm
3
does not terminate on initialization, and let
\varepsilon
be the output of the algorithm. Then the following hold:

(i)

If
d\lesssim\sigma
, then
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\asymp d\lesssim\varepsilon
.

(ii)

If
d\gtrsim\sigma
and
\sigma\geq\varepsilon
, then
\varepsilon\asymp\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\asymp\sigma\wedge d
.

(iii)

If
d\gtrsim\sigma
and
\sigma\leq\varepsilon
, we have
\varepsilon\asymp\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)
.

If Algorithm
3
does terminate on initialization,
\varepsilon\asymp\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)
.

Appendix B
Proofs for Section
1

Lemma B.1

(Generic Lower Bound on the Minimax Rate)
.

For a closed convex body
K
with diameter
d
, we have that
\varepsilon^{*2}\gtrsim\min(w(K)^{2}/n,\sigma^{2}\cdot w(K)^{2}/d^{2})
. This bound is sharp for an Euclidean ball.

Proof of Lemma
B.1
.

Define the spherical width
w^{s}(K)
of
K
as
\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(\vec{0},\mathbb{I}_{n})}\sup_{t\in K}\langle t,\xi/\|\xi\|\rangle
.
The Dvoretzky-Milman Theorem states that given a convex body
K
, for
m\lesssim\eta^{2}w(K)^{2}/d^{2}
, a random projection
P
onto an
m
-dimensional subspace satisfies
(1-\eta)B\subset PK\subseteq(1+\eta)B
, where
B
is a ball of radius
w^{s}(K)\asymp w(K)/\sqrt{n}

[
Vershynin, 2018
, Lemma 7.5.6, Lemma 7.6.2, Exercise 11.3.9]
. It follows that the minimax rate is always at least
\min(w(K)^{2}/n,w(K)^{2}/d^{2}\sigma^{2})
. This is because one can select a point
(Pv,P^{\perp}v)
for
v\in K
such that
Pv
is the center of a
(1-\eta)B
(so that when we draw a ball of radius
\varepsilon<w^{s}(K)
its projection will be completely in the ball
(1-\eta)B
). Then if
\varepsilon\lesssim\min(w(K)/\sqrt{n},w(K)/d\sigma)
, we can construct a packing set by just taking points in the sphere
(Pv^{i},P^{\perp}v^{i})
so that
\|v^{i}-v^{j}\|\geq\|Pv^{i}-Pv^{j}\|\geq\varepsilon/c
. These points will be at least exponential in the dimension which is
\exp(cw(K)^{2}/d^{2})
, and hence since
\varepsilon/\sigma\leq\sqrt{w(K)^{2}/d^{2}}
we have that
\varepsilon^{*2}\gtrsim\min(w(K)^{2}/n,w(K)^{2}/d^{2}\sigma^{2})
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
1.4
.

First suppose
d\geq\sigma
. Take
\kappa>\max(2,\sqrt{1/\log 2}).
Then taking
\varepsilon=\sigma/\kappa\leq d/\kappa\leq d/2
, we can place a ball of radius
\varepsilon
centered at a point in
K
where there exists a diameter of that ball of length
2\varepsilon<d
contained inside
K
. By picking equispaced points along the diameter of this ball, we conclude that
\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)>\log 2
(provided
c^{\ast}
is sufficiently large). So we have
\varepsilon^{2}/\sigma^{2}=1/\kappa^{2}<\log 2<\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)
. Thus,
\varepsilon^{\ast}\geq\sigma/\kappa
by its definition as a supremum, so that
\varepsilon^{\ast}\gtrsim\sigma\geq\sigma\wedge d
. Suppose on the other hand that
d<\sigma
. Then fitting a diameter (of some ball) of length
\varepsilon=d/3
inside
K
, we can ensure
\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)>\log 2
while also having

\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}=\frac{d^{2}}{9\sigma^{2}}<\frac{\sigma^{2}}{9\sigma^{2}}=\frac{1}{9}<\log 2.

This proves
\varepsilon^{\ast}\geq d/3,
so that
\varepsilon^{\ast}\gtrsim d\geq\sigma\wedge d
. In either case,
\varepsilon^{\ast}\gtrsim\sigma\wedge d
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
1.5
.

Define
S(C_{1},C_{2})=\{\varepsilon>0:\varepsilon^{2}/\sigma^{2}\leq C_{1}\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(C_{2}\varepsilon)\}
. Our goal is to show that
\sup S(C_{1},C_{2})\asymp\sup S(1,1)
for all
C_{1},C_{2}>0
.

First, we prove that
\sup S(1,C_{2})\asymp\sup S(1,1)
for all
C_{2}>0
. Suppose
C_{2}<1
. Pick
\varepsilon\in S(1,C_{2})
, so that
\varepsilon^{2}/\sigma^{2}\leq\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(C_{2}\varepsilon).
Then multiplying by
C_{2}^{2}
and using
C_{2}<1
,

\tfrac{(C_{2}\varepsilon)^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\leq C_{2}^{2}\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(C_{2}\varepsilon)<\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(C_{2}\varepsilon).

Thus
C_{2}\varepsilon\in S(1,1)
, so
\sup S(1,1)\geq C_{2}\varepsilon
. Since this holds for any
\varepsilon\in S(1,C_{2})
, we have
\sup S(1,1)\gtrsim\sup S(1,C_{2})
. On the other hand, pick
\varepsilon\in S(1,1)
. Since
\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)
is non-increasing in
\varepsilon
, we have

\tfrac{\varepsilon^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\leq\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)\leq\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(C_{2}\varepsilon),

proving that
\varepsilon\in S(1,C_{2})
. As this shows
S(1,1)\subseteq S(1,C_{2})
, we have
\sup S(1,C_{2})\geq\sup S(1,1)
. This proves that
\sup S(1,C_{2})\asymp\sup S(1,1)
when
C_{2}<1
.

Suppose instead that
C_{2}\geq 1
. Then picking any
\varepsilon\in S(1,C_{2})
, we have by the non-increasing property of
\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)
that

\tfrac{\varepsilon^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\leq\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(C_{2}\varepsilon)\leq\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon).

Then
\varepsilon\in S(1,1)
, showing that
S(1,C_{2})\subseteq S(1,1)
and thus
\sup S(1,1)\geq\sup S(1,C_{2})
. On the other hand, pick
\varepsilon\in S(1,1)
so that
\varepsilon^{2}/\sigma^{2}\leq\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)
. Then

\tfrac{(\varepsilon/C_{2})^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\leq\tfrac{\varepsilon^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\leq\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)=\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(C_{2}\cdot\varepsilon/C_{2}).

Hence
\varepsilon/C_{2}\in S(1,C_{2})
, so that
\sup S(1,C_{2})\geq\varepsilon/C_{2}
. This holds for all
\varepsilon\in S(1,1)
, so
\sup S(1,C_{2})\gtrsim\sup S(1,1)
, proving that
\sup S(1,C_{2})\asymp\sup S(1,1)
when
C_{2}\geq 1
.

We have thus proved that
\sup S(1,C_{2})\asymp\sup S(1,1)
for all
C_{2}>0
. It suffices to now prove that
\sup S(C_{1},C_{2})\asymp\sup S(1,C_{2})
for all
C_{1},C_{2}>0
.

As before, first suppose
C_{1}<1
. That
S(C_{1},C_{2})\subseteq S(1,C_{2})
is clear since
C_{1}\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(C_{2}\varepsilon)\leq\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(C_{2}\varepsilon),
so we have
\sup S(1,C_{2})\geq\sup S(C_{1},C_{2})
. On the other hand, if
\varepsilon\in S(1,C_{2})
, then by the non-increasing property of
\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}
,

\displaystyle\tfrac{(\sqrt{C_{1}}\varepsilon)^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{C_{1}\varepsilon^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\leq C_{1}\cdot\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(C_{2}\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(C_{2}(\sqrt{C_{1}}\varepsilon)).

Thus
\sqrt{C_{1}}\varepsilon\in S(C_{1},C_{2})
, so
\sup S(C_{1},C_{2})\geq\sqrt{C_{1}}\varepsilon
for all
\varepsilon\in S(1,C_{2})
. This proves
\sup S(C_{1},C_{2})\gtrsim\sup S(1,C_{2})
, so that
\sup S(1,C_{2})\asymp\sup S(C_{1},C_{2})
when
C_{1}<1
.

Finally, if
C_{1}\geq 1
, we have
S(1,C_{2})\subseteq S(C_{1},C_{2})
since
\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(C_{2}\varepsilon)\leq C_{1}\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(C_{2}\varepsilon)
so that
\sup S(C_{1},C_{2})\geq\sup S(1,C_{2})
. On the other hand, for any
\varepsilon\in S(C_{1},C_{2})
, we have

\tfrac{(\varepsilon/\sqrt{C_{1}})^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}=\tfrac{1}{C_{1}}\cdot\tfrac{\varepsilon^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\leq\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(C_{2}\varepsilon).

Hence
\varepsilon/\sqrt{C_{1}}\in S(1,C_{2})
, proving that
\sup S(1,C_{2})\geq\varepsilon/\sqrt{C_{1}}
for all
\varepsilon\in S(C_{1},C_{2})
. So
\sup S(1,C_{2})\gtrsim\sup S(C_{1},C_{2})
and hence
\sup S(1,C_{2})\asymp\sup S(C_{1},C_{2})
when
C_{1}\geq 1
.

This proves
\sup S(C_{1},C_{2})\asymp\sup S(1,C_{2})
for all
C_{1},C_{2}>0
and we already showed
\sup S(1,C_{2})\asymp S(1,1)
for all
C_{2}>0
. This proves that
\varepsilon^{{\dagger}}=\sup S(C_{1},C_{2})\asymp\sup S(1,1)=\varepsilon^{\ast}
for all
C_{1},C_{2}>0
.
∎

Appendix C
Proofs for Section
2

Proof of Lemma
2.1
.

We will need the following restatement of
Chatterjee [2014, Theorem 1.1]
(to include an arbitrary
\sigma
).

Theorem C.1

(Theorem 1.1 of
Chatterjee [2014]
)
.

For any
x\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|-\varepsilon_{\mu,w}|\geq x\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mu,w}})

\displaystyle\leq 3\exp\left(-\frac{x^{4}}{32\sigma^{2}(1+x/\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mu,w}})^{2}}\right).

(C.1)

We now prove the lemma. Let
C=\int_{0}^{\infty}6x\exp(-x^{4}/(32(1+x)^{2}))\mathrm{d}x>1
.
Suppose now
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}\geq 4C\sigma>\sigma
. Then by (
C.1
) we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|-\varepsilon_{\mu,w}|\geq x\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mu,w}})

\displaystyle\leq 3\exp\left(-\frac{x^{4}}{32\sigma^{2}(1+x/\sqrt{\sigma})^{2}}\right),

and hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|-\varepsilon_{\mu,w})^{2}/\varepsilon_{\mu,w}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}6x\exp\left(-\frac{x^{4}}{32\sigma^{2}(1+x/\sqrt{\sigma})^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=C\sigma.

Rearranging and using the fact that
\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|^{2}}
by Jensen’s inequality, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|^{2}-2\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|^{2}}\varepsilon_{\mu,w}+\varepsilon_{\mu,w}^{2}-C\sigma\varepsilon_{\mu,w}\leq 0.

Thus
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}-\sqrt{C\sigma\varepsilon_{\mu,w}}\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|^{2}}\leq\varepsilon_{\mu,w}+\sqrt{C\sigma\varepsilon_{\mu,w}}
.
Since by assumption
\sqrt{C\sigma\varepsilon_{\mu,w}}\leq\varepsilon_{\mu,w}/2
we conclude that
\varepsilon^{2}_{\mu}/4\leq\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|^{2}\leq 9\varepsilon^{2}_{\mu}/4
.

On the other hand, suppose
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}<4C\sigma
. Then setting
z=x\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mu,w}}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|-\varepsilon_{\mu,w}|\geq z)

\displaystyle\leq 3\exp\left(-\frac{z^{4}}{32\sigma^{2}(\varepsilon_{\mu,w}+z)^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 3\exp\left(-\frac{z^{4}}{32\sigma^{2}(4C\sigma+z)^{2}}\right).

Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|-\varepsilon_{\mu,w})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}6z\exp\left(-\frac{z^{4}}{32\sigma^{2}(4C\sigma+z)^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}6t\exp\left(-\frac{t^{4}}{32(4C+t)^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}\sigma^{2}.

Thus we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|^{2}-2\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|^{2}}\varepsilon_{\mu,w}+\varepsilon_{\mu,w}^{2}-C^{\prime}\sigma^{2}\leq 0.

which implies that
\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|^{2}}\leq\varepsilon_{\mu,w}+\sqrt{C^{\prime}}\sigma\leq C^{\prime\prime}\sigma
, which completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.3
.

To prove our first claim, it suffices to show for any
\mu\in K
that
\sigma\mapsto\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)
is non-decreasing on
[0,\infty)
. We first consider the case where
K
is not a singleton set and restrict to
\sigma>0
. We must first verify some technicalities about the subgradient. Fix
\mu\in K
and pick any
\sigma>0
. Let
\partial w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)
be the subgradient of the map
\varepsilon\mapsto w_{\mu}(\varepsilon).
This mapping is proper (i.e., finite everywhere) and concave, so the subgradient will exist in the interior of the domain of
w_{\nu}(\varepsilon)
which is
(0,\infty)
.

We now verify that
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)
is in the interior of this domain, i.e.,
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)>0
. To see this, observe that
w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)\gtrsim\varepsilon\wedge d>0
by
Vershynin [2018, Proposition 7.5.2(vi)]
. Then for sufficiently small
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma\cdot w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)-\varepsilon^{2}/2

\displaystyle\gtrsim\sigma\cdot\varepsilon-\varepsilon^{2}/2>0

\displaystyle=\sigma\cdot w_{\mu}(0)-0^{2}/2.

In other words,
0\not\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\varepsilon}[\sigma\cdot w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)-\varepsilon^{2}/2]
so
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)>0
. This ensures
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)
is in the interior of the domain of
w_{\nu}(\varepsilon)
, i.e.,
(0,\infty)
, and the subgradient exists at this point. Moreover, since
\varepsilon\mapsto w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)
is concave, the subgradient is indeed monotone non-increasing in
\varepsilon
.

We now prove
\sigma\mapsto\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)
is non-decreasing on
(0,\infty)
. Observe that
\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\varepsilon}[\sigma\cdot w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)-\varepsilon^{2}/2]
is achieved at
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)
which satisfies
\sigma\cdot\partial w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)=\varepsilon
.
Suppose
0<\sigma<\sigma^{\prime}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sigma^{\prime}\cdot\partial w_{\mu}(\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma^{\prime}))\geq\sigma^{\prime}\cdot\partial w_{\mu}(\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma))

\displaystyle\geq\sigma\cdot\partial w_{\mu}(\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma))=\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma).

Thus, the map
\sigma\mapsto\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)
is non-decreasing on
(0,\infty)
so long as
K
is not a singleton set.

Let us now handle the case where
K
is a singleton set and show the mapping is non-decreasing on
(0,\infty)
. Well, we must have
w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)=0
for any
\varepsilon\geq 0
. Hence

\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\varepsilon}[\sigma w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)-\varepsilon^{2}/2]=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\varepsilon}[-\varepsilon^{2}/2]=0.

(C.2)

This holds for any
\sigma
, hence
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)=0\leq 0=\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma^{\prime})
for any
0<\sigma<\sigma^{\prime}
. Our map is thus non-decreasing on
(0,\infty)
without the non-singleton restriction.

Let us now show the non-decreasing property extends to
[0,\infty)
. We again start with the non-singleton case for
K
. By similar logic to (
C.2
), we have
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(0)=0
. Next, for
\sigma^{\prime}>0
, recall we showed previously that
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma^{\prime})>0
, i.e., lies in the interior of the domain of
w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)
. Hence
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma^{\prime})>\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(0)
. This proves
\sigma\mapsto\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)
is non-decreasing on
[0,\infty)
provided
K
is not a singleton.

Finally, we suppose
K
is a singleton set and show the mapping is non-decreasing on
[0,\infty)
. We still have
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(0)=0
, and by our previous argument in (
C.2
),
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma^{\prime})=0
for any
\sigma^{\prime}>0
. Thus, trivially,
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(0)\leq\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma^{\prime})
for any
\sigma^{\prime}>0
, verifying
\sigma\mapsto\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)
is non-decreasing on
[0,\infty)
regardless of the singleton status of
K
. This completes our proof that the map
\sigma\mapsto\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)
is non-decreasing on
[0,\infty)
.

For our next claim, let
c>1
. If either
\sigma=0
or
K
is a singleton set, the desired inequality is trivial. Otherwise, suppose
\sigma>0
and
K
is not a singleton set. We have established that for any
\mu
, the subgradient is monotone non-increasing in
\varepsilon
. Using our first order subgradient condition
\sigma\cdot\partial w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)=\varepsilon
and the fact that
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(c\sigma)\geq\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)
, it follows that

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(c\sigma)

\displaystyle=c\sigma\cdot\partial w_{\mu}(\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(c\sigma))\leq c\sigma\cdot\partial w_{\mu}(\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma))

\displaystyle=c\cdot\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma).

Taking the
\sup
over
\mu\in K
, we have
\varepsilon_{K,w}(c\sigma)\leq c\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)
, and since we already showed
\sigma\mapsto\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)
is non-decreasing, we also have
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\leq\varepsilon_{K,w}(c\sigma)
.

Suppose instead
0\leq c<1
. The
c=0
case is trivial, so assume
c\in(0,1)
. Using our previous result applied to
c^{-1}>1
,

\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)=\varepsilon_{K,w}(c^{-1}\cdot c\sigma)\leq c^{-1}\varepsilon_{K,w}(c\sigma),

or equivalently
c\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\leq\varepsilon_{K,w}(c\sigma)
. We also have
\varepsilon_{K,w}(c\sigma)\leq\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)
again since
\sigma\mapsto\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)
is non-decreasing. This proves our final claim.
∎

C.1
Proofs for Section
2.1

Proof of Proposition
2.9
.

Let
\varepsilon_{K^{\prime};K,w}=\sup_{\mu\in K^{\prime}}\varepsilon_{\mu,w}
, where
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\varepsilon}\sigma w_{K,\mu}(\varepsilon)-\varepsilon^{2}/2
. For brevity, we set
\overline{\varepsilon}=\overline{\varepsilon_{K^{\prime};K}}
. Observe now that for every
\mu\in K^{\prime}
, we have
\sigma\cdot w_{K,\mu}(2\overline{\varepsilon})-2\overline{\varepsilon}^{2}<0
, and thus by
Chatterjee [2014, Proposition 1.3]
we have
2\overline{\varepsilon}>\varepsilon_{K^{\prime};K,w}
.

We will now relate
\varepsilon_{K^{\prime};K,w}
to
{\varepsilon_{K^{\prime};K,\mathrm{LS}}}
.

Case 1:
Suppose
\varepsilon_{K^{\prime};K,w}\gtrsim\sigma
. Pick
\tilde{\mu}\in K^{\prime}
that maximizes
\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\tilde{\mu}\|^{2}
.

Case 1(a):
Suppose
\varepsilon_{\tilde{\mu},w}\gtrsim\sigma
. Then by Lemma
2.1
and definition of
\varepsilon_{K^{\prime};K,w}
, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{K^{\prime};K,\mathrm{LS}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\tilde{\mu}\|^{2}\asymp\varepsilon_{\tilde{\mu},w}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{K^{\prime};K,w}^{2}<4\overline{\varepsilon}^{2}

as required.

Case 1(b):
Suppose
\varepsilon_{\tilde{\mu},w}\lesssim\sigma
. Then by Lemma
2.1
and our assumption for Case 1,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{K^{\prime};K,\mathrm{LS}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\tilde{\mu}\|^{2}\lesssim\sigma^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{K^{\prime};K,w}^{2}<4\overline{\varepsilon}^{2}.

Case 2
: Suppose
\varepsilon_{K^{\prime};K,w}\lesssim\sigma
. This means for any
\mu\in K^{\prime}
,
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}\lesssim\sigma
. Hence
\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|^{2}\lesssim\sigma^{2}
by Lemma
2.1
for all
\mu\in K^{\prime}
, so that
\varepsilon_{K^{\prime};K,\mathrm{LS}}\lesssim\sigma
. We now consider two subcases.

Case 2(a):
Suppose
d\geq\frac{2\sigma(n+1)}{n\sqrt{2\pi}}\gtrsim\sigma
, where
d=\mathrm{diam}(K^{\prime})
. We show this implies
\overline{\varepsilon}\gtrsim\sigma
which proves
\varepsilon_{K^{\prime};K,\mathrm{LS}}\lesssim\overline{\varepsilon}
.

Note that
K^{\prime}
contains a diameter of length
2\sigma/\kappa
(for a sufficiently large absolute constant
\kappa
). Thus, by
Vershynin [2018, Proposition 7.5.2(vi)]
,

\sup_{\mu\in K^{\prime}}w_{K,\mu}(\sigma/\kappa)\geq\sup_{\mu\in K^{\prime}}w_{K^{\prime},\mu}(\sigma/\kappa)\geq\frac{2\sigma}{\kappa\sqrt{2\pi}}.

Since for sufficiently large
\kappa
we have
\sigma/(2\kappa^{2})\leq 2\sigma/(\kappa\sqrt{2\pi})
, it follows that
\sup_{\mu\in K^{\prime}}w_{K,\mu}(\varepsilon)\geq\varepsilon^{2}/2\sigma
for
\varepsilon=\sigma/\kappa
. By definition of
\overline{\varepsilon}
as a supremum of such
\varepsilon
, we have
\overline{\varepsilon}\geq\sigma/\kappa
. This completes the case when
d\gtrsim\sigma
.

Case 2(b):
Suppose
d\leq\frac{2\sigma(n+1)}{n\sqrt{2\pi}}
. Then by the fact that
K^{\prime}\subseteq K
followed by Jung’s Theorem and
Vershynin [2018, Proposition 6.5.2(vi)]
, for some
\mu\in K^{\prime}
we have

\displaystyle w_{K,\mu}\left(\sqrt{\tfrac{n}{2(n+1)}}d\right)

\displaystyle\geq w_{K^{\prime},\mu}\left(\sqrt{\tfrac{n}{2(n+1)}}d\right)=w(K^{\prime})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{d}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\geq\frac{d}{2\sqrt{2\pi}}.

Notice that
\frac{d}{2\sqrt{2\pi}}\leq\frac{nd^{2}}{2(n+1)}\cdot\frac{1}{2\sigma}
so we have
w_{K,\mu}(\varepsilon)\geq\varepsilon^{2}/(2\sigma)
for
\varepsilon=\sqrt{\frac{nd^{2}}{2(n+1)}}
. Therefore
\overline{\varepsilon}\gtrsim d
, and as
\varepsilon_{K^{\prime};K,\mathrm{LS}}\lesssim d,
we have
\varepsilon_{K^{\prime};K,\mathrm{LS}}\lesssim\overline{\varepsilon}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.11
.

To argue this, we will use the well known technique for the reverse Sudakov minoration estimate
[see the proof of Theorem 8.1.13 of
Vershynin, 2018
, e.g.]
. We will spell out the details due to our use of a different dyadic scale (multiplying by
c^{\ast}
rather than
2
) and use of local packings instead of global ones.

Fix
\mu\in K
. We start at level
\varepsilon
and build an
\varepsilon/c^{\ast}
maximal packing set
\mathcal{P}_{1}
of
B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K
. Next for any point
\mu_{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}
, we build an
\varepsilon/{c^{\ast}}^{2}
packing set of
B(\mu_{i},\varepsilon/c^{\ast})\cap K
. The totality of these packing set points at the second level comprise
\mathcal{P}_{2}
, and
|\mathcal{P}_{2}|\leq\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})
. We then continue this process. Note that on the
k
th level, we have obtained an
\varepsilon/{c^{\ast}}^{k}
covering set
\mathcal{P}_{k}
of
B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K
. This can be seen by induction. For the first level, this follows since
\mathcal{P}_{1}
is a maximal
\varepsilon/c^{\ast}
packing of the set. Suppose the covering claim is true for level
k-1
. Since
\mathcal{P}_{k-1}
forms a covering, any point
\nu\in B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K
will fall in an
\varepsilon/{c^{\ast}}^{k-1}
ball centered at some point in
\mathcal{P}_{k-1}
. To construct
\mathcal{P}_{k}
, we must at perform a maximal
\varepsilon/{c^{\ast}}^{k}
-packing (hence covering) of this ball intersected with
K
, among others. Hence
\nu
will be within distance
\varepsilon/{c^{\ast}}^{k}
to a point in
\mathcal{P}_{k}
. Another inductive argument proves
|\mathcal{P}_{k}|\leq\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon/{c^{\ast}}^{i})
.

We now perform the chaining argument. Let
k
be defined as the maximum integer so that
\varepsilon{c^{\ast}}^{-k}\geq\frac{w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)}{2c^{\ast}\sqrt{n}}
. By
Vershynin [2018, Proposition 7.5.2(vi)]
we have

w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)\geq\frac{\mathrm{diam}(B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K)}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\geq\frac{2\varepsilon\wedge d}{\sqrt{2\pi}},

where
d=\operatorname{diam}(K)
. It follows that
k\leq\log_{c^{\ast}}\frac{\varepsilon 2c^{\ast}\sqrt{2\pi n}}{2\varepsilon\wedge d}
.
We now write

\displaystyle w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)=\mathbb{E}\sup_{\nu\in B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K}\langle\xi,\nu\rangle=\mathbb{E}\sup_{\nu\in B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K}\langle\xi,\nu-\mu\rangle,

and recognize
\langle\xi,\nu-\mu\rangle=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\langle\xi,\nu_{j}-\nu_{j-1}\rangle+\langle\xi,\nu-\nu_{k}\rangle
, where
\nu_{0}=\mu
, and
\nu_{j}
is the closest point to
\nu
from the
j
th packing set which we have constructed. Thus it suffices to bound

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\sup_{\nu\in B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K}\langle\xi,\nu_{j}-\nu_{j-1}\rangle+\mathbb{E}\sup_{\nu\in B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K}\langle\xi,\nu-\nu_{k}\rangle.

(C.3)

Consider each term in the summation. The covering property implies

\displaystyle\|\nu_{j}-\nu_{j-1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nu_{j}-\nu\|+\|\nu-\nu_{j-1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon/{c^{\ast}}^{j}+\varepsilon/{c^{\ast}}^{j-1}

\displaystyle\leq(1+c^{\ast})\varepsilon/{c^{\ast}}^{j}.

Thus, using a Gaussian maximal inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{\nu\in B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K}\langle\xi,\nu_{j}-\nu_{j-1}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon}{{c^{\ast}}^{j}}\sqrt{\log\prod_{l=0}^{j-1}\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon/{c^{\ast}}^{l})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon}{{c^{\ast}}^{j}}\sqrt{j\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon/{c^{\ast}}^{j-1})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon}{{c^{\ast}}^{j}}\sqrt{k\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon/{c^{\ast}}^{j-1})},

where we used the fact that
\varepsilon\mapsto\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)
is non-increasing
[
Neykov, 2022
, Lemma II.8]
and
C>1
is an absolute constant (depending on
c^{\ast}
, which is itself a sufficiently large fixed constant).

For the second term in (
C.3
), Cauchy-Schwarz and the definition of
k
imply

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{\nu\in B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K}\langle\xi,\nu-\nu_{k}\rangle\leq\frac{\varepsilon\cdot\mathbb{E}\|\xi\|}{{c^{\ast}}^{k}}\leq\frac{\varepsilon\sqrt{n}}{{c^{\ast}}^{k}}\leq\frac{w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)}{2}.

Rearranging,

\displaystyle w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\leq 2C\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{\varepsilon}{c^{\ast}\cdot{c^{\ast}}^{j-1}}\sqrt{k\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon/{c^{\ast}}^{j-1})}

\displaystyle\leq 2Ck^{3/2}\sup_{\delta\leq\varepsilon}\frac{\delta}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\delta)}

\displaystyle\leq 2C\bigg({\log_{c^{\ast}}\frac{\varepsilon 2c^{\ast}\sqrt{2\pi n}}{2\varepsilon\wedge d}}\bigg)^{3/2}\sup_{\delta\leq\varepsilon}\frac{\delta}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\delta)}.

Note this holds for all
\mu\in K
.
Observe that if
\varepsilon
is such that the right-hand side above is smaller than
\varepsilon^{2}/(2\sigma)
we will have an upper bound on
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}
(up to constant factors) by Proposition
2.4
. For brevity, set
C_{n}=4C\bigg({\log_{c^{\ast}}c^{\ast}\sqrt{2\pi n}}\bigg)^{3/2}
. Set
\varepsilon=2C_{n}\underline{\varepsilon}
, and assume at first that
4C_{n}\underline{\varepsilon}\leq d
. This implies
2C\bigg({\log_{c^{\ast}}\frac{\varepsilon 2c^{\ast}\sqrt{2\pi n}}{2\varepsilon\wedge d}}\bigg)^{3/2}\leq C_{n}/2
. To control
w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)
, it thus suffices to show

\displaystyle\frac{C_{n}}{2}\sup_{\delta\leq\varepsilon}\frac{\delta}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\delta)}\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma}.

By definition of
\underline{\varepsilon}
, we know
(2\underline{\varepsilon})^{2}/(2\sigma)\geq\frac{1}{2}\sup_{\delta\leq 2\underline{\varepsilon}}\delta/c^{\ast}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\delta)}
, so

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma}=\frac{(2C_{n}\underline{\varepsilon})^{2}}{2\sigma}\geq\frac{C_{n}^{2}}{2}\sup_{\delta\leq 2\underline{\varepsilon}}\frac{\delta}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\delta)}.

Now

\displaystyle\sup_{\delta\leq 2C_{n}\underline{\varepsilon}}\frac{\delta}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\delta)}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\delta\leq 2\underline{\varepsilon}}\frac{\delta}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\delta)}\vee\sup_{2\underline{\varepsilon}<\delta\leq 2C_{n}\underline{\varepsilon}}\frac{\delta}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\delta)}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\delta\leq 2\underline{\varepsilon}}\frac{\delta}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\delta)}\vee\frac{2C_{n}\underline{\varepsilon}}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(2\underline{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{n}\sup_{\delta\leq 2\underline{\varepsilon}}\frac{\delta}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\delta)},

where we are using
C_{n}\geq 1
and that for convex sets the map
\varepsilon\mapsto\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)}
is non-increasing
[
Neykov, 2022
, Lemma II.8]
. Hence we showed

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma}\geq\frac{C_{n}}{2}\sup_{\delta\leq 2C_{n}\underline{\varepsilon}}\delta/c^{\ast}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\delta)}.

On the other hand, if
4C_{n}\underline{\varepsilon}>d
, we already know that
d
is an upper bound on the rate.
∎

C.2
Proofs for Section
2.2

Proof of Theorem
2.14
.

We first prove that
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)/c\leq\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\leq\overline{\varepsilon}(4c\sigma/(c-1))
, where recall
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)=\sup_{\mu\in K}\varepsilon_{\mu,w}
.
Pick
\mu\in K
and
\nu^{\ast}\in B(\mu,\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma))\cap K
achieving the supremum and infimum, respectively, in the definition of
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)
. Then

\displaystyle w_{\nu^{\ast}}((2+1/c)\bar{\varepsilon}(\sigma))-\frac{(2+1/c)^{2}\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)^{2}}{2\sigma}

\displaystyle\geq w_{\mu}(\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma))-\frac{(2+1/c)^{2}\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)^{2}}{2\sigma}

\displaystyle\geq w_{\nu^{\ast}}(\bar{\varepsilon}(\sigma)/c)-\frac{(\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)/c)^{2}}{2\sigma}.

The first inequality follows since
B(\mu,\bar{\varepsilon}(\sigma))\subset B(\nu^{\ast},(2+1/c)\bar{\varepsilon}(\sigma))
and the second from the definition of
\bar{\varepsilon}(\sigma)
.
Hence by concavity of
\varepsilon\mapsto w_{\nu^{\ast}}(\varepsilon)-\varepsilon^{2}/(2\sigma)
, we have
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\geq\varepsilon_{\nu^{ast},w}(\sigma)\geq\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)/c
.

Now define
\sigma^{\prime}:=4c\sigma/(c-1)>\sigma
. Then for all
\mu\in K
and all
\kappa>0
,

\displaystyle w_{\mu}((1+\kappa)\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime}))-w_{\mu}((1+\kappa)\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})/c)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(4+4/c)(1+\kappa)^{2}\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})^{2}}{2\sigma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-1/c^{2})(1+\kappa)^{2}\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})^{2}}{2\sigma}.

Thus

\displaystyle w_{\mu}((1+\kappa)\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime}))-\frac{(1+\kappa)^{2}\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})^{2}}{2\sigma}

\displaystyle\leq w_{\mu}((1+\kappa)\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})/c)-\frac{(1/c^{2})(1+\kappa)^{2}\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})^{2}}{2\sigma}.

Again by concavity of
\varepsilon\mapsto w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)-\varepsilon^{2}/(2\sigma)
, we have
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)\leq(1+\kappa)\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})
. Since this holds for all
\mu\in K
,
we have
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\leq(1+\kappa)\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})
. Taking
\kappa\rightarrow 0
implies
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\leq\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})
. Thus,

\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)/c\leq\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\leq\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime}).

(C.4)

Now we proceed to bounding
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}
.

Case 1:
Suppose
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
. Then
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
by (
C.4
). Then picking
\mu\in K
that maximizes
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)
, we have
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
, so that by Lemma
2.1
,
\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|^{2}\asymp\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)^{2}=\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)^{2}
. Since
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}^{2}\geq\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|^{2}
, we use (
C.4
) to conclude
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\gtrsim\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\geq\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)/c
. It remains to show for Case 1 that
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\lesssim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})
. Well, let
\tilde{\mu}
maximize
\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\tilde{\mu}\|^{2}
.

Case 1(a):
If
\varepsilon_{\tilde{\mu},w}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
, then by Lemma
2.1
and (
C.4
) ,

\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\tilde{\mu}\|^{2}\asymp\varepsilon_{\tilde{\mu},w}(\sigma)^{2}\leq\sup_{\mu}\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)^{2}=\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)^{2}\lesssim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})^{2}.

Case 1(b):
If
\varepsilon_{\tilde{\mu},w}(\sigma)\lesssim\sigma
, then by Lemma
2.1
and our earlier fact that
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
,

\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\tilde{\mu}\|^{2}\lesssim\sigma^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)^{2}\lesssim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})^{2}.

This proves in Case 1 that
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)/c\lesssim\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\lesssim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime}).

Case 2:
Now suppose
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\lesssim\sigma
. Then
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\lesssim\sigma
by (
C.4
). Then for all
\mu\in K
, we have
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)\lesssim\sigma
which in turn implies for all
\mu\in K
that
\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|^{2}\lesssim\sigma^{2}
by Lemma
2.1
. Thus
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\lesssim\sigma
, and that
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\lesssim d
is clear. Hence
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\lesssim\sigma\wedge d
. Then by Lemma
1.4
and the minimax rate being a lower bound on the worst case LSE rate, we have
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\gtrsim\varepsilon^{\ast}\gtrsim\sigma\wedge d
. Thus,
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\asymp\sigma\wedge d
when
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\lesssim\sigma
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.15
.

We abbreviate
\overline{\varepsilon}=\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)
but if we mean
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})
we always explicitly write out the
\sigma^{\prime}
.

Case 1:
Suppose
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
. We first upper bound
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)
. Take
\varepsilon^{\prime}=(1+\kappa)\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})
where
\kappa>0
. For any
\mu\in K
, by definition of
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\nu_{1}\in B(\mu,\varepsilon^{\prime})\cap K}w_{\nu_{1}}(\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})-\inf_{\nu_{2}\in B(\mu,\varepsilon^{\prime})\cap K}w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{\prime 2}}{2\sigma^{\prime}}-\frac{L\varepsilon^{\prime}}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon^{\prime})}.

By
Talagrand [2014, Exercise 2.4.11]
,

w_{\mu}(\varepsilon^{\prime})\leq\sup_{\nu_{1}\in B(\mu,\varepsilon^{\prime})\cap K}w_{\nu_{1}}(\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})+(\tfrac{L}{c^{\ast}})\varepsilon^{\prime}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon^{\prime})}.

Moreover, clearly

\inf_{\nu_{2}\in B(\mu,\varepsilon^{\prime})\cap K}w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})\leq w_{\mu}(\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast}).

Combining these three bounds yields

\displaystyle w_{\mu}(\varepsilon^{\prime})-w_{\mu}(\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{\prime 2}}{2\sigma^{\prime}}.

Using the definition of
\sigma^{\prime}
, this implies

\displaystyle w_{\mu}(\varepsilon^{\prime})-\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime 2}}{2\sigma}\leq w_{\mu}(\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})-\frac{(\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})^{2}}{2\sigma},

so that
\varepsilon^{\prime}\geq\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)
by our concavity argument again. Taking
\kappa\rightarrow 0
shows that
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\geq\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)
. Since this holds for all
\mu\in K
, we have
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\geq\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)
(this fact will also hold for Case 2 by this argument). Note that
\sigma^{\prime}>\sigma
, and so if
\varepsilon
satisfies the condition in the definition of
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)
, then it satisfies the condition in the analogous definition of
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})
. Thus,
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\geq\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)
. Since this case assumed
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
, we have
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\gtrsim\sigma
.

Now, taking
\tilde{\mu}
to maximize
\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\tilde{\mu}\|
, if
\varepsilon_{\tilde{\mu},w}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
, then by Lemma
2.1
and our upper bound for
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)
,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\tilde{\mu}\|^{2}\asymp\varepsilon_{\tilde{\mu},w}(\sigma)^{2}\leq\sup_{\mu}\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)^{2}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)^{2}\lesssim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})^{2}.

On the other hand, if
\varepsilon_{\tilde{\mu},w}(\sigma)\lesssim\sigma
, then by Lemma
2.1
we have

\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\tilde{\mu}\|^{2}\leq\sigma^{2}\lesssim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})^{2}.

This completes the proof that if
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
, then
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\lesssim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})
.

Let us now prove our lower bound
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\gtrsim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)
, which we accomplish by comparing
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)
to
\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
, defined in (
2.3
). Recall from Remark
2.16
that
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\geq\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
.

Case 1(a):
Suppose
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\geq 2\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
. Then using the definition of
\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
along with the non-increasing property of
\eta\mapsto\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\eta)
established in
Neykov [2022, Lemma II.8]
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{C^{2}(\overline{\varepsilon}/2)^{2}}{4\sigma^{2}}\geq\left(\tfrac{L}{c^{\ast}}\right)^{2}\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\overline{\varepsilon}/2)\geq(\tfrac{L}{c^{\ast}})^{2}\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\overline{\varepsilon}).

Taking square roots and multiplying by
\overline{\varepsilon}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{C\overline{\varepsilon}^{2}}{2\sigma}\geq\frac{2L\overline{\varepsilon}}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\overline{\varepsilon})},

so that

\displaystyle\frac{C\overline{\varepsilon}^{2}}{2\sigma}-\frac{L\overline{\varepsilon}}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\overline{\varepsilon})}\geq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{C\overline{\varepsilon}^{2}}{2\sigma}.

(C.5)

Now pick
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}
that achieves the supremum in the definition of
\overline{\varepsilon}
, using the fact that
B(\nu_{1},\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})\subseteq B(\nu_{2},2\overline{\varepsilon}+\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})
to conclude
w_{\nu_{2}}(2\overline{\varepsilon}+\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})\geq w_{\nu_{1}}(\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})
. Using this fact along with our definition of
\overline{\varepsilon}
, (
C.5
), and
C
, we obtain

\displaystyle w_{\nu_{2}}(2\overline{\varepsilon}+\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})-\frac{(2+1/c^{\ast})^{2}\overline{\varepsilon}^{2}}{2\sigma}

\displaystyle\geq w_{\nu_{1}}(\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})-\frac{(2+1/c^{\ast})^{2}\overline{\varepsilon}^{2}}{2\sigma}

\displaystyle\geq w_{\nu_{2}}(\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})-\frac{(\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})^{2}}{2\sigma}.

This implies by concavity of
\varepsilon\mapsto w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon)-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma}
that
\varepsilon_{\nu_{2},w}(\sigma)\geq\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)/c^{\ast}
. Thus, since
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
, we have
\varepsilon_{\nu_{2},w}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
. By Lemma
2.1
,
\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\nu_{2}\|^{2}\asymp\varepsilon_{\nu_{2},w}(\sigma)^{2}\geq\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)^{2}/{c^{\ast}}^{2}
. Taking the supremum, we have
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\gtrsim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)
as claimed.

Case 1(b):
Suppose
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\in(\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast},2\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast})
. Then by Lemma
1.5
applied to
\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
, we have
\overline{\varepsilon}\asymp\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}\asymp\varepsilon^{\ast}
, i.e.,
\overline{\varepsilon}
is the minimax rate up to constants. In this case,
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\gtrsim\overline{\varepsilon}
, since the minimax rate is in particular upper-bounded by the LSE rate
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)
.

To summarize Case 1, when
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
, we have
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\lesssim\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\lesssim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})
.

Case 2:
Suppose
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\lesssim\sigma
. Then since
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\geq\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)
(proved in Case 1 without using the
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\gtrsim\sigma
assumption) we have
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\lesssim\sigma
. Well if for all
\mu\in K
we have
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}\lesssim\sigma
, we have by Lemma
2.1
that
\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|^{2}\lesssim\sigma^{2}
for all
\mu\in K
. Thus,
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\lesssim\sigma
. Clearly
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\lesssim d
, so
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\lesssim\sigma\wedge d
.

That
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma\wedge d
again follows from Lemma
1.4
and the minimax rate being a lower bound on the worst case LSE rate. Thus
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\asymp\sigma\wedge d
when
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\lesssim\sigma
.
∎

Lemma C.2
.

For every
\varepsilon
, the map
\nu\mapsto w_{\nu}(\varepsilon)
is concave over
K
.
3
3

3

What is more, one can easily see that the map
(\varepsilon,\nu)\mapsto w_{\nu}(\varepsilon)
is concave. The proof is nearly identical to the proof of the original statement so we omit it. We will however use this result later on.

Proof.

Let
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\in K
and
\nu_{3}=\alpha\nu_{1}+(1-\alpha)\nu_{2}\in K
. Then

B(\nu_{3},\varepsilon)\cap K\supset\alpha(B(\nu_{1},\varepsilon)\cap K)+(1-\alpha)(B(\nu_{2},\varepsilon)\cap K),

so
Vershynin [2018, Proposition 7.5.2(iv)]
implies

\displaystyle w(B(\nu_{3},\varepsilon)\cap K)

\displaystyle\geq w(\alpha(B(\nu_{1},\varepsilon)\cap K)+(1-\alpha)(B(\nu_{2},\varepsilon)\cap K))

\displaystyle=\alpha w_{\nu_{1}}(\varepsilon)+(1-\alpha)w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon).

∎

Proof of Theorem
2.18
.

The structure of our proof is as follows: In Part I, we show that
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\lesssim\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)
when
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
. In Part II, we show that
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\gtrsim\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)
(where
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)
was defined following Lemma
2.1
). In Part III, we use the result of Part II to show that
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\lesssim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})
and also that if
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\lesssim\sigma
, then
{\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}}(\sigma)\asymp\sigma\wedge d
.

Part I
: We prove that
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\lesssim\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)
when
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
. For this direction, we abbreviate
\overline{\varepsilon}:=\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)
. Define

\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}:=\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}(\sigma):=\sup\{\varepsilon>0:\tfrac{C^{2}\varepsilon^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\leq(\tfrac{L}{c^{\ast}})^{2}\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)\}.

By Lemma
1.5
,
\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}\asymp\varepsilon^{\ast}
. By near identical reasoning to Remark
2.16
,
\overline{\varepsilon}\geq\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
(argue for
\varepsilon\leq\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
that
\tfrac{L}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)}\geq\tfrac{C\varepsilon}{\sigma}
, multiply by
\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|
, then compare
\varepsilon
to
\overline{\varepsilon}
). Thus we consider the cases where
\overline{\varepsilon}\in(\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast},2\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast})
and
\overline{\varepsilon}\geq 2\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
.

If
\overline{\varepsilon}\in(\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast},2\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast})
, then
\overline{\varepsilon}\asymp\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}\asymp\varepsilon^{\ast}
, i.e.,
\overline{\varepsilon}
is the minimax rate up to constants. Hence
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\gtrsim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)
as desired, since the minimax rate is a lower bound on the worst case LSE rate.

We consider the remaining case where
\overline{\varepsilon}\geq 2\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
. Then an argument similar to that used in Theorem
2.15
to derive (
C.5
) shows that
C\overline{\varepsilon}/\sigma>2(L/c^{\ast})\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\overline{\varepsilon})}
, and therefore

\displaystyle\frac{C\overline{\varepsilon}\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|}{\sigma}-\frac{L\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\overline{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle>\frac{C\overline{\varepsilon}\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|}{2\sigma}.

(C.6)

Next suppose that we can find
\nu_{1}
and
\nu_{2}
achieving the supremum in the definition of
\overline{\varepsilon}
. We consider two subcases and in both demonstrate that
\overline{\varepsilon}\lesssim\varepsilon_{\nu_{2},w}(\sigma)
.

Case 1:
Suppose
\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|>\overline{\varepsilon}
. Then we can find a point
\nu_{3}=\alpha\nu_{1}+(1-\alpha)\nu_{2}
such that
\|\nu_{3}-\nu_{2}\|=\alpha\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|=\overline{\varepsilon}
, i.e., by taking
\alpha=\frac{\overline{\varepsilon}}{\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|}\in(0,1)
. On the other hand, by the concavity of
\nu\mapsto w_{\nu}(\varepsilon)
(Lemma
C.2
),

\displaystyle w_{\nu_{3}}(\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})-w_{\nu_{2}}(\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})

\displaystyle\geq\alpha(w_{\nu_{1}}(\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})-w_{\nu_{2}}(\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast}))

\displaystyle\geq\frac{C\overline{\varepsilon}^{2}}{\sigma}-\frac{L\overline{\varepsilon}}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\overline{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{C\overline{\varepsilon}^{2}}{2\sigma},

(C.7)

where the second inequality used the definition of
\overline{\varepsilon}
and
\alpha
and the third used (
C.6
) (after multiplying by
\overline{\varepsilon}/\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|
). Thus,

\displaystyle w_{\nu_{2}}(\|\nu_{3}-\nu_{2}\|+\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})-\frac{(\|\nu_{3}-\nu_{2}\|+\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})^{2}}{2\sigma}

\displaystyle\geq w_{\nu_{3}}(\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})-\frac{(\|\nu_{3}-\nu_{2}\|+\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})^{2}}{2\sigma}

\displaystyle\geq w_{\nu_{2}}(\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})+\frac{C\overline{\varepsilon}^{2}}{2\sigma}-\frac{(\|\nu_{3}-\nu_{2}\|+\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})^{2}}{2\sigma}

\displaystyle=w_{\nu_{2}}(\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})+\frac{C\overline{\varepsilon}^{2}}{2\sigma}-\frac{(\overline{\varepsilon}+\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})^{2}}{2\sigma}

\displaystyle=w_{\nu_{2}}(\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})-\frac{(\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})^{2}}{2\sigma}.

The first inequality used
B(\nu_{3},\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})\subseteq B(\nu_{2},\|\nu_{3}-\nu_{2}\|+\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})
which allowed us to compare the Gaussian width terms. The second used (
C.7
) and the third that
\overline{\varepsilon}=\|\nu_{3}-\nu_{2}\|
. The final line holds since
C=1+\frac{2}{c^{\ast}}
. Then by concavity of
\varepsilon\mapsto w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon)-\varepsilon^{2}/(2\sigma)
, our resulting inequality shows that
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\geq\varepsilon_{\nu_{2},w}(\sigma)\geq\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)/c^{\ast}
.

Case 2:
Suppose
\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|\leq\overline{\varepsilon}
. Then we have

\displaystyle w_{\nu_{2}}(\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|+\tfrac{\overline{\varepsilon}}{c^{\ast}})-\frac{(\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|+\tfrac{\overline{\varepsilon}}{c^{\ast}})^{2}}{2\sigma}

\displaystyle\geq w_{\nu_{1}}(\tfrac{\overline{\varepsilon}}{c^{\ast}})-\frac{(\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|+\tfrac{\overline{\varepsilon}}{c^{\ast}})^{2}}{2\sigma}

\displaystyle\geq w_{\nu_{2}}(\tfrac{\overline{\varepsilon}}{c^{\ast}})+\frac{C\overline{\varepsilon}\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|}{\sigma}-\frac{L\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)}}{c^{\ast}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{(\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|+\tfrac{\overline{\varepsilon}}{c^{\ast}})^{2}}{2\sigma}

\displaystyle\geq w_{\nu_{2}}(\tfrac{\overline{\varepsilon}}{c^{\ast}})+\frac{C\overline{\varepsilon}\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|}{2\sigma}-\frac{(\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|+\tfrac{\overline{\varepsilon}}{c^{\ast}})^{2}}{2\sigma}

\displaystyle\geq w_{\nu_{2}}(\tfrac{\overline{\varepsilon}}{c^{\ast}})-\frac{(\tfrac{\overline{\varepsilon}}{c^{\ast}})^{2}}{2\sigma}.

The first inequality used
B(\nu_{1},\tfrac{\overline{\varepsilon}}{c^{\ast}})\subseteq B(\nu_{2},\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|+\tfrac{\overline{\varepsilon}}{c^{\ast}})
, the second used the definition of
\overline{\varepsilon}
, and the third our result (
C.6
). For the last inequality, we note that
\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|\leq\overline{\varepsilon}
implies that

\displaystyle\frac{C\overline{\varepsilon}\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|}{2\sigma}-\frac{(\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|+\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})^{2}}{2\sigma}

\displaystyle=\frac{\overline{\varepsilon}\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|}{\sigma}\cdot\underbrace{\left(\frac{C}{2}-\frac{1}{c^{\ast}}\right)}_{=1/2}-\frac{\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|^{2}}{2\sigma}-\frac{(\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})^{2}}{2\sigma}

\displaystyle=\frac{\overline{\varepsilon}\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|}{2\sigma}-\frac{\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|^{2}}{2\sigma}-\frac{(\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})^{2}}{2\sigma}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\overline{\varepsilon}\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|}{2\sigma}-\frac{\overline{\varepsilon}\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|}{2\sigma}-\frac{(\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})^{2}}{2\sigma}

\displaystyle=-\frac{(\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})^{2}}{2\sigma}.

Thus, we have
w_{\nu_{2}}(\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|+\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})-\frac{(\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|+\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})^{2}}{2\sigma}\geq w_{\nu_{2}}(\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})-\frac{(\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast})^{2}}{2\sigma}
which implies by concavity of
\varepsilon\mapsto w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon)-\varepsilon^{2}/(2\sigma)
that
\varepsilon_{\nu_{2},w}(\sigma)\geq\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)/c^{\ast}
. This completes Case 2.

Thus, we have shown for both Case 1 and Case 2 of the setting where
\overline{\varepsilon}\geq 2\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
that
\varepsilon_{\nu_{2},w}(\sigma)\geq\overline{\varepsilon}/c^{\ast}
. Now if
\overline{\varepsilon}\gtrsim\sigma
as we assumed, we have
\varepsilon_{\nu_{2},w}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
. Hence by Lemma
2.1

\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\nu_{2}\|^{2}\asymp\varepsilon_{\nu_{2},w}(\sigma)^{2}\gtrsim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)^{2}.

Taking the sup over
\nu_{2}
, we conclude
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\gtrsim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)
as desired.

This proves that when
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
, then no matter if
\overline{\varepsilon}\in(\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast},2\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast})
or
\overline{\varepsilon}\geq 2\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
(for which we had Case 1 and Case 2), we have
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\gtrsim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)
.

Part II.
Next, we prove that
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\gtrsim\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)
. Take
\varepsilon^{\prime}=(1+\kappa)\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})
where
\sigma^{\prime}=\frac{4C}{1-4/{c^{\ast}}^{2}}\cdot\sigma
and
\kappa>0
is an arbitrary constant. For any
\mu\in K
, consider the set
B(\mu,\varepsilon^{\prime})\cap K
. Pack the set
B(\mu,\varepsilon^{\prime})\cap K
maximally at distance
\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast}
. Choose the point
\nu_{1}
in the packing that maximizes
w_{\nu_{1}}(\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})
. By
Talagrand [2014, Exercise 2.4.11]
, we know that

w_{\mu}(\varepsilon^{\prime})\leq w_{\nu_{1}}(\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})+\frac{\tilde{L}\varepsilon^{\prime}}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon^{\prime})}

(C.8)

for a sufficiently large absolute constant
\tilde{L}
.

Similarly, pick
\nu_{2}
in the packing to minimize
w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})
. It should satisfy

w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})\leq w_{\mu}(2\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})

(C.9)

since if we took
\nu_{3}
as the point in the packing set closest to
\mu
, we have
w_{\nu_{3}}(\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})\leq w_{\mu}(2\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})
due to
B(\nu_{3},\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})\subseteq B(\mu,2\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})
. Thus
\nu_{2}
certainly satisfies (
C.9
). Observe also that we can always assume that
\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast}
since if the two coincide, we can take them to be any other two points. That is, unless the packing contains one single point which would imply that
\varepsilon^{\prime}\gtrsim\operatorname{diam}(K)\gtrsim\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}
, which in turn yields our claim that
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\gtrsim\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}
by taking
\kappa\to 0
. To fully resolve the remaining claims in the case when the packing is just a single point, if
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})<\sigma
, this means
\varepsilon^{\prime}\lesssim\sigma
so that
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\lesssim\sigma
. So
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\lesssim\sigma\wedge d
. But also
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\gtrsim\varepsilon^{\ast}\gtrsim\sigma\wedge d
by Lemma
1.4
, showing
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\asymp\sigma\wedge d
. We now return to the original case where
\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast}
. Further, since we are packing
B(\mu,\varepsilon^{\prime})\cap K
, we have
\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|\leq 2\varepsilon^{\prime}
. Additionally, by definition of
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})
as a supremum and the fact that
\varepsilon^{\prime}>\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})
,

\displaystyle w_{\nu_{1}}(\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})-w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon^{\prime}\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|}{\sigma^{\prime}}-\frac{L\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon^{\prime})}}{c^{\ast}}.

(C.10)

Combining (
C.8
), (
C.9
), and (
C.10
) along with
\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast}\leq\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|\leq 2\varepsilon^{\prime}
, we conclude that so long as
L\geq c^{\ast}\tilde{L}
, we have

\displaystyle w_{\mu}(\varepsilon^{\prime})-w_{\mu}(2\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})

\displaystyle=(w_{\mu}(\varepsilon^{\prime})-w_{\nu_{1}}(\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast}))+(w_{\nu_{1}}(\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})-w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\underbrace{(w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})-w_{\mu}(2\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast}))}_{\leq 0}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tilde{L}\varepsilon^{\prime}}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon^{\prime})}+\frac{C\varepsilon^{\prime}\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|}{\sigma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{L\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon^{\prime})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon^{\prime})}\cdot\left(\tilde{L}\varepsilon^{\prime}-L\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\underbrace{\frac{C\varepsilon^{\prime}\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|}{\sigma^{\prime}}}_{\leq 2C\varepsilon^{\prime 2}/\sigma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon^{\prime})}\cdot\underbrace{(\tilde{L}\varepsilon^{\prime}-L\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})}_{\leq 0}+\frac{2C\varepsilon^{\prime 2}}{\sigma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2C\varepsilon^{\prime 2}}{\sigma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime 2}}{2\sigma}-\frac{(2\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})^{2}}{2\sigma}.

This implies for our choice of
\sigma^{\prime}
that

\displaystyle w_{\mu}(\varepsilon^{\prime})-\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime 2}}{2\sigma}\leq w_{\mu}(2\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})-\frac{(2\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast})^{2}}{2\sigma}.

Using concavity of
\varepsilon\mapsto w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma}
and the fact that
\varepsilon^{\prime}>2\varepsilon^{\prime}/c^{\ast}
for
c^{\ast}>2
, we conclude
\varepsilon^{\prime}\gtrsim\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)
. Taking
\kappa\to 0
, we have
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\gtrsim\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)
. Since we took an arbitrary
\mu\in K
, we have
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\gtrsim\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)
.

Part III:
Finally, we show that
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\lesssim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})
and if
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\lesssim\sigma
, then
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\asymp\sigma\wedge d
.

We start with the first claim. Let
\tilde{\mu}\in K
maximize
\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\tilde{\mu}\|^{2}.
Suppose
\varepsilon_{\tilde{\mu},w}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
. Then using the definition of
\tilde{\mu}
, Lemma
2.1
, and the result of Part II, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\tilde{\mu}\|^{2}\asymp\varepsilon_{\tilde{\mu},w}(\sigma)^{2}\leq\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)^{2}\lesssim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})^{2}.

On the other hand, if
\varepsilon_{\tilde{\mu},w}(\sigma)\lesssim\sigma,
then by Lemma
2.1

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\tilde{\mu}\|^{2}\lesssim\sigma^{2}.

But also
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}^{2}\lesssim d^{2}
hence
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}^{2}\lesssim\sigma^{2}\wedge d^{2}
. Now recall from Part I that
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\geq\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}(\sigma^{\prime})
(we used the scaling
\sigma
rather than
\sigma^{\prime}
in Part I). Using these facts along with Lemma
1.4
to relate
\underline{\varepsilon}^{*}(\sigma^{\prime})
(which is the minimax rate up to constants) to
\sigma^{\prime}\wedge d
, we obtain

\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}^{2}\lesssim\sigma^{2}\wedge d^{2}\asymp{\sigma^{\prime}}^{2}\wedge d^{2}\lesssim(\underline{\varepsilon}^{*}(\sigma^{\prime}))^{2}\lesssim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})^{2}.

We now prove the final claim. If
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\lesssim\sigma
, we have from Part II that
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\lesssim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\lesssim\sigma
. By Lemma
2.1
(applied at every
\mu\in K
), this implies
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\lesssim\sigma
and since
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\leq d
as previously argued, we conclude
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\lesssim\sigma\wedge d
. Then again by Lemma
1.4
and the minimax rate being a lower bound on the worst case LSE rate, we have
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\gtrsim\varepsilon^{\ast}\gtrsim\sigma\wedge d
. Hence if
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\lesssim\sigma
, then
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\asymp\sigma\wedge d
. This concludes Part III, which in conjunction with Part I, completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Corollary
2.19
.

We first prove two intermediate claims. Let
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)
be as defined in Theorem
2.18
.

Claim 1:

\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\geq\varepsilon^{\ast}
. Proof: if
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon^{\ast}
, then
\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)}\geq\varepsilon/\sigma
by definition of
\varepsilon^{\ast}
. Assuming
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}
are chosen without loss of generality so that
w_{\nu_{1}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})\geq w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})
, we have

\displaystyle w_{\nu_{1}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-w_{\nu_{2}}(\tfrac{\varepsilon}{c^{\ast}})-\frac{C\varepsilon\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|}{\sigma}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{L\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)}

\displaystyle\geq w_{\nu_{1}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-\frac{C\varepsilon\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|}{\sigma}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{L\varepsilon\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|}{c^{\ast}\sigma}

\displaystyle=w_{\nu_{1}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})+\frac{\overline{C}\varepsilon\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|}{\sigma}

\displaystyle\geq 0,

provided
L
is sufficiently large. This implies the condition in the definition of
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)
holds, so
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\geq\varepsilon
. This holds for all
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon^{\ast}
, so we conclude
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\geq\varepsilon^{\ast}
.

Claim 2:
Suppose the map
\mu\mapsto w_{\mu}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})
has a Lipschitz constant
\overline{C}\varepsilon/\sigma
for all
\varepsilon>\varepsilon^{\ast}
for some constant
\overline{C}
. Then
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\lesssim\varepsilon^{\ast}
. Proof: If
\varepsilon>\varepsilon^{\ast}
, then
(\varepsilon/\sigma)\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|\geq\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)}
by definition of
\varepsilon^{\ast}
. Hence for sufficiently small
\overline{C}
, we have

\displaystyle|w_{\nu_{1}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\overline{C}\varepsilon}{\sigma}\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma}\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|-\frac{L\varepsilon}{c^{\ast}\sigma}\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{\sigma}\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|-\frac{L}{c^{\ast}}\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)}.

Then we have
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\leq\varepsilon
by definition of
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)
in Theorem
2.18
, but since this holds for all
\varepsilon>\varepsilon^{\ast}
, this implies
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\leq\varepsilon^{\ast}
.

Using these claims, let us prove our main claim. Suppose the map has a Lipschitz constant
\varepsilon/\sigma
up to constants for all
\varepsilon\gtrsim\varepsilon^{\ast}
. Now let
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma\cdot\tfrac{4C}{1-4/{c^{\ast}}^{2}}\asymp\sigma
. Let
\varepsilon^{\ast}(\sigma^{\prime})
be as defined in (
1.2
) but with
\sigma^{\prime}
replacing
\sigma
. By Lemma
1.5
,
\varepsilon^{\ast}\asymp\varepsilon^{\ast}(\sigma^{\prime})
. Thus, we conclude that the map has a Lipschitz constant
\varepsilon/\sigma^{\prime}
(up to constants) for all
\varepsilon\gtrsim\varepsilon^{\ast}(\sigma^{\prime})
. Then combining Claim 1 and Claim 2, we know
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\asymp\varepsilon^{\ast}(\sigma^{\prime})\asymp\varepsilon^{\ast}
. But by the theorem we have
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\lesssim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma^{\prime})\asymp\varepsilon^{\ast}
. Thus the LSE is minimax optimal.

Conversely, suppose the LSE is minimax optimal. Suppose
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
. Then by Theorem
2.18
,
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\lesssim\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\asymp\varepsilon^{\ast}
. Note that
\mu\mapsto w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)
must be
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)/\sigma
-Lipschitz (up to constants) by definition of
\overline{\varepsilon}
. Then since increasing the Lipschitz constant preserves the Lipschitz property, we know the map is
\varepsilon^{\ast}/\sigma
-Lipschitz. Hence, for any
\varepsilon\gtrsim\varepsilon^{\ast}
, the map is
\varepsilon/\sigma
-Lipschitz.

Suppose instead
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\lesssim\sigma
, so that the mapping has a Lipschitz constant
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)/\sigma\lesssim 1
. If we can show
\varepsilon^{\ast}\gtrsim\sigma
, so that
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)/\sigma\lesssim 1\lesssim\varepsilon^{\ast}/\sigma
, then the mapping will also be Lipschitz with constant
\varepsilon^{\ast}/\sigma
and hence
\varepsilon/\sigma
-Lipschitz for any
\varepsilon>\varepsilon^{\ast}
. Well, by Claim 1,
\varepsilon^{\ast}\lesssim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\lesssim\sigma
. But also
\varepsilon^{\ast}\lesssim d
, so
\varepsilon^{\ast}\lesssim\sigma\wedge d
. We also know from Lemma
1.4
that
\varepsilon^{\ast}\gtrsim\sigma\wedge d
, so in fact
\varepsilon^{\ast}\asymp\sigma\wedge d
. Now if
\sigma\lesssim d
, then this means
\varepsilon^{\ast}\asymp\sigma
, so certainly
\varepsilon^{\ast}\gtrsim\sigma
. Suppose
\sigma\gtrsim d
, so that
\varepsilon^{\ast}\asymp d
. Well observe that if
\varepsilon\gtrsim\varepsilon^{\ast}
, i.e.,
\varepsilon\gtrsim d
(with a constant larger than
1
), then

|w_{\nu_{1}}(\varepsilon)-w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon)|=|w(K)-w(K)|=0\leq\tfrac{\varepsilon}{\sigma}\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|_{2}.

Here we used that
B(\nu_{1},d)\cap K=B(\nu_{2},d)\cap K=K
. Hence the mapping is
\varepsilon/\sigma
-Lipschitz for all
\varepsilon\gtrsim\varepsilon^{\ast}
as required.
∎

Appendix D
Proofs for Section
3

D.1
Proofs for Section
3.1

Proof of Lemma
3.2
.

We will take
a=0,b=1
for simplicity. Suppose
\varepsilon\gtrsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
. Set
k=\varepsilon\sqrt{n}>1
and assume for simplicity both
k
and
n/k=\sqrt{n}/\varepsilon
are integers. Construct the vector

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle=(\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{k},\underbrace{\tfrac{k}{n},\ldots,\tfrac{k}{n}}_{k},\underbrace{\tfrac{2k}{n},\ldots,\tfrac{2k}{n}}_{k},\ldots,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\underbrace{1-\tfrac{k}{n},\ldots,1-\tfrac{k}{n}}_{k})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Now consider perturbing this vector by

\displaystyle v_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=(\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{k},\underbrace{\tfrac{\alpha_{1}k}{n},\ldots,\tfrac{\alpha_{1}k}{n}}_{k},\underbrace{\tfrac{\alpha_{2}k}{n},\ldots,\tfrac{\alpha_{2}k}{n}}_{k},\ldots,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\underbrace{\tfrac{\alpha_{n/k-2}k}{n},\ldots,\tfrac{\alpha_{n/k-2}k}{n}}_{k},\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{k}),

where the vector
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\ldots,\alpha_{n/k-2})\in\{0,1\}^{n/k-2}
, noting that
u+v_{\alpha}
is monotonic and has components in
[0,1]
. By Varshamov-Gilbert’s bound, there exists at least
\exp((n/k-2)/8)
vectors from
\{0,1\}^{n/k-2}
