such that the Hamming distance between any of them is at least
(n/k-2)/4
. Given
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{n/k-2}
with this property, note that

\displaystyle\|v_{\alpha}-v_{\alpha^{\prime}}\|^{2}\geq k(\varepsilon/\sqrt{n})^{2}(n/k-2)/8\gtrsim\varepsilon^{2}.

Therefore, there exist at least
\exp((n/k-2)/8)\asymp\exp(\sqrt{n}/\varepsilon)
vectors
u+v_{\alpha}
that form an
\varepsilon
-packing set of
S^{\uparrow}(a,b)
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.4
.

We present the
p>2
case only, with the
p=2
case identical except we swap the choice of upper bound from
Gao and Wellner [2007, Theorem 1.1]
. Let
\|\cdot\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}
denote the
L^{2}
-norm of a function on
\mathbb{R}^{p}
with respect to the Lebesgue measure in
p
-dimensions.

We begin with
a=0
and
b=1
. Let
\varepsilon>0
. Take
\delta=\frac{\varepsilon}{2\sqrt{n}}
. By Lemma
3.3
, there exists a
\delta
-covering
\mathcal{G}_{p}
of
\mathcal{F}_{p}
with
|\mathcal{G}_{p}|\leq C\delta^{-2(p-1)}
.

Now, for each
\mu\in Q_{0,1}\subseteq[0,1]^{n}
, we construct a corresponding
f^{\mu}\in\mathcal{F}_{p}
. We must partition the domain of
f^{\mu}
, i.e.,
[0,1]^{p}
. Let

\displaystyle I^{-}

\displaystyle=\{x=(x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{p}):x\in[0,1]^{p},

\displaystyle\qquad\exists i\in\{1,\dots,p\}\text{ such that }x_{i}=0\}.

The
n
unique elements
l_{1},\dots,l_{n}
of
\mathbb{L}_{p,n}
define
n
cubes that partition
(0,1]^{p}
as follows. Given
l_{j}=\left(\frac{k_{1}^{j}}{n^{1/p}},\dots,\frac{k_{p}^{j}}{n^{1/p}}\right)
for
k_{1}^{j},\dots,k_{p}^{j}\in\{1,\dots,n^{1/p}\}
, define the corresponding cube
I_{j}=\prod_{i=1}^{p}\left(\frac{k_{i}^{j}-1}{n^{1/p}},\frac{k_{i}^{j}}{n^{1/p}}\right]
that has
l_{j}
as its outermost corner. Then
[0,1]^{p}=I^{-}\cup\bigcup_{j=1}^{n}I_{j}
is a disjoint partition. Thus, we define for all
x\in[0,1]^{p}
the function

f^{\mu}(x)=\begin{cases}\mu_{j}&\text{ if }x\in I_{j}\\
0&\text{ if }x\in I^{-}.\end{cases}

Let us verify that
f^{\mu}
is indeed an element of
\mathcal{F}_{p}
. Clearly
f^{\mu}(x)
lies in
[0,1]
for all
x\in[0,1]^{p}
. Let
e^{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
be the unit vector with
1
in coordinate
i
and 0 in all other coordinates. To prove monotonicity, let
x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{p})\in[0,1]^{p}
and consider the following cases.

Suppose
x\in I^{-}
. Pick any
\alpha>0
and coordinate
s\in\{1,\dots,p\}
such that
x+\alpha e^{s}\in[0,1]^{p}
(such an
\alpha
and
s
must exist since
x
is not the all-ones vector). Then
x+\alpha e^{s}
is in some cube
I_{j}
or still in
I^{-}
, so
f^{\mu}
takes value
\mu_{j}\geq 0
or
0
, both options are
\geq 0=f^{\mu}(x)
(preserving monotonicity).

Suppose
x\in I_{j}
for some
j
, i.e.,
x_{i}\in\left(\frac{k_{i}^{j}-1}{n^{1/p}},\frac{k_{i}^{j}}{n^{1/p}}\right]
for each
1\leq i\leq p
. Then
f^{\mu}(x)=\mu_{j}
. Assume
x
is not the all-ones vector. Now again consider
x+\alpha e^{s}
for some
s\in\{1,\dots,p\}
where
\alpha>0
and
x+\alpha e^{s}\in[0,1]^{p}
. Then
x+\alpha e^{s}
belongs to some cube
I_{t}
where
l_{t}
has the same coordinates as
l_{j}
except a possibly larger
s
th coordinate, and
f^{\mu}(x+\alpha e^{s})=\mu_{t}
. Since
\mu\in Q_{0,1}
, for some
g\in\mathcal{F}_{p}
,
\mu_{t}=g(l_{t})
and
\mu_{j}=g(l_{j})
. But since
l_{t}
and
l_{j}
differ only in the
s
th coordinate and
g
is non-decreasing in each coordinate, we conclude
\mu_{j}\leq\mu_{t}
. So
f^{\mu}(x)\leq f^{\mu}(x+\alpha e^{s})
. If
x
is the all-ones vector, then repeat the same logic but compare with
x-\alpha e^{s}\in[0,1]^{p}
. This verifies that
f^{\mu}\in\mathcal{F}_{p}
, so we have a well-defined mapping from
Q_{0,1}
to
\mathcal{F}_{p}
.

Recalling our
\delta
-covering
\mathcal{G}_{p}
, for each
g\in\mathcal{G}_{p}
, if it exists, pick a single
\mu(g)\in Q_{0,1}
such that
\|f^{\mu(g)}-g\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}<\delta
. We then produce a subset
Q_{0,1}^{\prime}=\{\mu(g):g\in\mathcal{G}\},
with
|Q_{0,1}^{\prime}|\leq|\mathcal{G}_{p}|\leq C\delta^{-2(p-1)}
.

Let us verify that
Q_{0,1}^{\prime}
is indeed an
\varepsilon
-covering of
Q_{0,1}
. Pick any
\mu\in Q_{0,1}
. Then
f^{\mu}
is an element of
\mathcal{F}_{p}
, and since
\mathcal{G}_{p}
is a
\delta
-covering of
\mathcal{F}_{p}
, there exists
g\in\mathcal{G}_{p}
such that
\|f^{\mu}-g\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}<\delta
. This also implies the existence of
\mu(g)
in
Q_{0,1}^{\prime}
such that
\|f^{\mu(g)}-g\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}<\delta
, possibly equal to
\mu
itself. By the triangle inequality,
\|f^{\mu}-f^{\mu(g)}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}<2\delta
. Note that
f^{\mu}
and
f^{\mu(g)}
are constant on each cube
I_{1},\dots,I_{n}
and each of these cubes has volume
1/n
. Moreover,
I^{-}
has volume
0
with respect to the Lebesgue measure. Hence

\displaystyle\underbrace{\|f^{\mu}-f^{\mu(g)}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}}_{<4\delta^{2}}

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1]^{p}}(f^{\mu}(x)-f^{\mu(g)}(x))^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{I_{j}}(f^{\mu}(x)-f^{\mu(g)}(x))^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu(x)

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}[\mu_{j}-(\mu(g))_{j}]^{2}

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\|\mu-\mu(g)\|_{2}^{2}.

Thus,
\|\mu-\mu(g)\|_{2}\leq 2\sqrt{n}\delta=\varepsilon
, so
Q_{0,1}^{\prime}
is indeed an
\varepsilon
-covering of
Q_{0,1}
of cardinality bounded by
C\delta^{-2(p-1)}=C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2\sqrt{n}}\right)^{-2(p-1)}
. In the case
p=2
, we instead have
|Q_{0,1}^{\prime}|\leq C\delta^{-2}(\log 1/\delta)^{2}=C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2\sqrt{n}}\right)^{-2}\left(\log\frac{2\sqrt{n}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{2}
. We have therefore upper-bounded the size of a minimal
\varepsilon
-covering of
Q_{0,1}
.

Now we consider arbitrary
a<b
, and again assume
p>2
. Let
\varepsilon>0
. Consider the linear function
\phi(x)=\frac{x}{b-a}-\frac{a}{b-a}
that satisfies
\phi(a)=0
and
\phi(b)=1
and has inverse
\phi^{-1}(x)=(b-a)x+a
. Let
\Phi\colon\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
apply
\phi
coordinate-wise, and let
\Phi^{-1}\colon\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
apply
\phi^{-1}
coordinate-wise instead. Observe that
\Phi(Q_{a,b})\subseteq Q_{0,1}
since if
f\colon[0,1]^{p}\to[a,b]
is non-decreasing in each variable, then
\phi\circ f\colon[0,1]^{p}\to[0,1]
is still non-decreasing in each variable and thus belongs to
\mathcal{F}_{p}
. Therefore, using our earlier result, there exists an
\frac{\varepsilon}{b-a}
-covering
Q_{a,b}^{\prime}
of
Q_{0,1}
and therefore of
\Phi(Q_{a,b})
with
|Q_{a,b}^{\prime}|\leq C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2\sqrt{n}(b-a)}\right)^{-2(p-1)}
.

Now observe that
\Phi^{-1}(Q_{a,b}^{\prime})
is a subset of
Q_{a,b}
, with cardinality
|\Phi^{-1}(Q_{a,b}^{\prime})|\leq C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2\sqrt{n}(b-a)}\right)^{-2(p-1)}
. Let us verify that
\Phi^{-1}(Q_{a,b}^{\prime})
is an
\varepsilon
-covering of
Q_{a,b}
. Pick any
\mu\in Q_{a,b}
. Then for some
f\colon[0,1]^{p}\to[a,b]
non-decreasing in each variable,
\mu=(f(l_{1}),\dots,f(l_{n}))
. Then
\Phi(\mu)\in\Phi(Q_{a,b})
so there is some
\eta\in Q_{a,b}^{\prime}
with
\|\Phi(\mu)-\eta\|_{2}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{b-a}
. Applying the definition of
\Phi
and rearranging shows
\|\mu-\Phi^{-1}(\eta)\|_{2}\leq\varepsilon
, and
\Phi^{-1}(\eta)
belongs to the claimed covering set
\Phi^{-1}(Q_{a,b}^{\prime})
.

The
p=2
case is similar, and we instead obtain a covering of cardinality upper-bounded by
C\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2\sqrt{n}(b-a)}\right)^{-2}\left(\log\frac{2\sqrt{n}(b-a)}{\varepsilon}\right)^{2}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.5
.

We first prove the claim for
a=0
and
b=1
. We re-use the notation from the proof of Lemma
3.4
, recalling our cubes
\mathcal{I}_{n}=\{I_{1},\dots,I_{n}\}
of side lengths
1/n^{1/p}
and volume
1/n
. We will show for
\varepsilon\gtrsim 1/n^{1/p}
that
\mathcal{M}\left(\sqrt{\frac{c_{p}n\varepsilon}{16}},Q_{0,1}\right)\gtrsim 2^{c_{p}\varepsilon^{1-p}/2}
where
c_{p}
is some constant depending on
p
. Performing a change of variables, this will imply
\log\mathcal{M}\left(\varepsilon,Q_{0,1}\right)\gtrsim\left(\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2\sqrt{n}}\right)^{-2(p-1)}
for
\varepsilon\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{n}{n^{1/p}}}
as desired.

Assume
\varepsilon\gtrsim 1/n^{1/p}
. We assume
\varepsilon=2^{-m}
for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
, noting that
\varepsilon<1
. For simplicity, we assume
n^{1/p}=k2^{m}
for some
k\in\mathbb{N}
, i.e.,
\varepsilon=k/n^{1/p}
. This means the cubes in
\mathcal{I}_{n}
have side lengths
2^{-m}/k
. Let’s instead partition
[0,1]^{p}
into a coarser set of cubes by taking cubes with side length
2^{-m}
instead of
2^{-m}/k
. To do so, define
\varepsilon^{-p}=2^{mp}
cubes
\mathcal{J}_{m}=\{J_{1},\dots,J_{2^{mp}}\}
of the form
\prod_{i=1}^{p}(r_{i}\varepsilon,(r_{i}+1)\varepsilon]
where
r_{i}\in\{0,1,\dots,2^{m}-1\}
. Each cube in
\mathcal{J}_{m}
contains exactly
k^{p}=n/2^{mp}\in\mathbb{N}
cubes of
\mathcal{I}_{n}
, and the boundary of cubes in
\mathcal{J}_{m}
are a subset of boundaries of cubes in
\mathcal{I}_{n}
. Note that
[0,1]^{p}
can be partitioned as
I^{-}\cup\bigcup_{i=1}^{2^{mp}}J_{i}
, where
I^{-}
was defined in the proof of Lemma
3.4
.

Now, we partition the cubes in
\mathcal{J}^{m}
as follows: let
\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}
be the set of cubes with
\sum_{i=1}^{p}r_{i}=2^{m}
,
\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}^{+}
the cubes with
\sum_{i=1}^{p}r_{i}>2^{m}
, and
\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}^{-}
the cubes with
\sum_{i=1}^{p}r_{i}<2^{m}
.

Consider the set
\mathcal{G}_{p}
of functions
g
on
[0,1]^{p}
such that
g
is either
1
or
0
in each cube in
\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}
and
g
is identically
0
on the cubes in
\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}^{-}
and identically 1 on the cubes in
\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}^{+}
. We set
g
to be value
0
on the set
I^{-}
. Note that
|\mathcal{G}_{p}|=2^{|\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}|}
.

Now let us prove that
\mathcal{G}_{p}\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{p}
. Clearly
g\in\mathcal{G}_{p}
takes values in
[0,1]
. To show the non-decreasing property, it suffices to enumerate all possible pairs of points where
g
takes value
0
on one point and
1
on the other and argue this does not violate monotonicity. First, consider
g
taking value
0
on a cube in
\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}^{-}
and
1
on a cube in
\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}^{+}
. Well if
g
is
1
on a cube
\prod_{i=1}^{p}(r_{i}\varepsilon,(r_{i}+1)\varepsilon]\in\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}^{+}
with
\sum_{i=1}^{p}r_{i}>2^{m}
, moving from that cube in the (positive) direction of coordinate
s
will never lead to a cube in
\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}^{-}
for any
s
since the value of
r_{i}
can not decrease. So we cannot obtain the needed comparable point to conclude monotonicity was violated. By near identical logic,
g
taking value
0
in
\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}^{-}
and
1
on a cube in
\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}
cannot lead to a violation, and neither will taking value
0
in
\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}
and
1
on
\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}^{+}
. Next, a violation within
\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}
cannot occur. This is because if we are at a cube with
\sum_{i=1}^{p}r_{i}=2^{m}
(where
g
is either
0
or
1
) and move along the positive
s
coordinate for any
s
until a new cube is reached, the new cube will have its defining coordinates satisfying
\sum_{i=1}^{p}r_{i}^{\prime}>2^{m}
. But
g
will be
1
on this new cube, meaning no violation occurred. Lastly,
g
taking value
0
on
I^{-}
does not cause an issue. For if
g
takes value
1
at a point, it does not belong to
I^{-}
, i.e., that point does not have a 0 coordinate. But moving in the positive direction in coordinate
s
will preserve this fact for any
s
, so we cannot arrive at
I^{-}
, i.e., a violation cannot occur. Hence
\mathcal{G}_{p}\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{p}
.

We can show
|\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}|\geq c_{p}\cdot\varepsilon^{1-p}
, where
c_{p}
is a constant depending only on
p
. To see this, note that
|\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}|
is the number of integer tuples
(r_{1},\dots,r_{p})
with
\sum_{i=1}^{p}r_{i}=2^{m}=\varepsilon^{-1}
and
0\leq r_{i}\leq 2^{m-1}
. Setting
r_{i}^{\prime}=1+r_{i}
, this is equivalent to counting the number of integer tuples
(r_{1}^{\prime},\dots,r_{p}^{\prime})
with
\sum_{i=1}^{p}r_{i}^{\prime}=2^{m}+p
and
1\leq r_{i}^{\prime}\leq 2^{m}
. By a standard stars-and-bars combinatorial argument,
|\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}|=\binom{2^{m}+p-1}{p-1}=\binom{\varepsilon^{-1}+p-1}{p-1}
. Then using a well-known binomial coefficient bound,

\displaystyle|\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{(\varepsilon^{-1}+p-1)^{p-1}}{(p-1)^{p-1}}\geq\frac{(\varepsilon^{-1})^{p-1}}{(p-1)^{p-1}}.

Indeed,
|\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}|\geq c_{p}\cdot\varepsilon^{1-p}
with
c_{p}
only depending on
p
as claimed.

Observe that there is a bijective correspondence between
\mathcal{G}_{p}
and
\{0,1\}^{|\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}|}
. We apply a Varshamov-Gilbert type bound to this set of binary strings, and equivalently
\mathcal{G}_{p}
, following the formulation of
Rigollet and Hütter [2023, Lemma 4.12]
with
\gamma=1/4
. The lemma states that there exists
g_{1},\dots,g_{N}\in\mathcal{G}_{p}
with
N\geq 2^{|\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}|/(32\log 2)}\geq 2^{c_{p}\varepsilon^{1-p}/(32\log 2)}
such that for any
i\neq j
, the functions
g_{i},g_{j}
disagree on at least
\frac{|\overline{\mathcal{J}}_{m}|}{4}\geq\frac{c_{p}\varepsilon^{1-p}}{4}
many cubes in
\mathcal{J}_{m}
. Let
\mathcal{J}_{m}^{i,j}
be these cubes for each
i\neq j
, so that
|\mathcal{J}_{m}^{i,j}|\geq\frac{c_{p}\varepsilon^{1-p}}{4}
. Since each cube of
\mathcal{J}_{m}
is partitioned into cubes of
\mathcal{I}_{n}
, we can define
\mathcal{I}_{n}^{i,j}
as the cubes of
\mathcal{I}_{n}
on which
g_{i}
and
g_{j}
disagree, so that the cubes of
\mathcal{J}_{m}^{i,j}
and
\mathcal{I}_{n}^{i,j}
both partition the same region.

Each
g_{i}
defines a unique point
\mu_{i}=(g_{i}(l_{1}),\dots,g_{i}(l_{n}))\in Q_{0,1}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
where
l_{1},\dots,l_{n}
are the lattice points. Let us compute
\|\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}\|_{2}
for
i\neq j
to verify we have formed a packing set of
Q_{0,1}
. Note that
g_{i}-g_{j}
is constant within any cube in
\mathcal{I}_{n}
, the difference being either
0
or
1
. By counting the number of cubes such that
g_{i}(l)\neq g_{j}(l)
, we count the number of non-zero coordinates of
\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}
. Recalling that each cube in
\mathcal{I}_{n}
has volume
n^{-1}
and each cube in
\mathcal{J}_{m}
volume
\varepsilon^{p}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\|\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}\|_{2}^{2}}{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{n}^{i,j}}\int_{I}\underbrace{|g_{i}(x)-g_{j}(x)|^{2}}_{=1}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\sum_{J\in\mathcal{J}_{m}^{i,j}}\int_{J}\underbrace{|g_{i}(x)-g_{j}(x)|^{2}}_{=1}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=|\mathcal{J}_{m}^{i,j}|\cdot\varepsilon^{p}\geq\frac{c_{p}\varepsilon^{1-p}}{4}\cdot\varepsilon^{p}=\frac{c_{p}\varepsilon}{4}.

Therefore,
\|\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}\|_{2}\geq\sqrt{\frac{c_{p}n\varepsilon}{4}}
. This implies
\mathcal{M}\left(\sqrt{\frac{c_{p}n\varepsilon}{4}},Q_{0,1}\right)\geq 2^{c_{p}\varepsilon^{1-p}/(32\log 2)}
for
\varepsilon\gtrsim\frac{1}{n^{1/p}}
, so that

\log\mathcal{M}\left(\sqrt{\frac{c_{p}n\varepsilon}{4}},Q_{0,1}\right)\geq\frac{c_{p}\varepsilon^{1-p}}{32}.

Perform a change of variables with
\tilde{\varepsilon}=\sqrt{\frac{c_{p}n\varepsilon}{4}}
which satisfies
\tilde{\varepsilon}\gtrsim\sqrt{n/n^{1/p}}
. Then

\log\mathcal{M}\left(\tilde{\varepsilon},Q_{0,1}\right)\geq\frac{c_{p}}{32}\cdot\left(\frac{4\tilde{\varepsilon}^{2}}{c_{p}n}\right)^{1-p}\gtrsim\left(\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}}{2\sqrt{n}}\right)^{-2(p-1)}.

Since
\varepsilon\gtrsim 1/n^{1/p}
if and only if
\tilde{\varepsilon}\gtrsim\sqrt{n/n^{1/p}}
, we have proven for all
\tilde{\varepsilon}\gtrsim\sqrt{n/n^{1/p}}
that
\log\mathcal{M}\left(\tilde{\varepsilon},Q_{0,1}\right)\gtrsim\left(\frac{\tilde{\varepsilon}}{2\sqrt{n}}\right)^{-2(p-1)}
.

Now we consider arbitrary
a<b
, and again assume
p\geq 2
. Suppose
\frac{\varepsilon}{b-a}\gtrsim\sqrt{n/n^{1/p}}
. Consider the linear function
\phi(x)=\frac{x}{b-a}-\frac{a}{b-a}
which satisfies
\phi(a)=0
and
\phi(b)=1
and has inverse
\phi^{-1}(x)=(b-a)x+a
. Let
\Phi\colon\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
apply
\phi
in each coordinate, and
\Phi^{-1}\colon\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
apply
\phi^{-1}
instead. It is easy to verify that
\Phi(Q_{a,b})=Q_{0,1}
. Then by our prior result, there is some set
Q_{a,b}^{\prime}\subseteq\Phi(Q_{a,b})
with
\log|Q_{a,b}^{\prime}|\geq\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2\sqrt{n}(b-a)}\right)^{-2(p-1)}
such that for any
\mu,\mu^{\prime}\in Q_{a,b}^{\prime}
, we have
\|\mu-\mu^{\prime}\|_{2}\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{b-a}.

Now observe that
\Phi^{-1}(Q_{a,b}^{\prime})
is a subset of
Q_{a,b}
, with cardinality satisfying
\log|\Phi^{-1}(Q_{a,b}^{\prime})|\geq\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2\sqrt{n}(b-a)}\right)^{-2(p-1)}
. Moreover, by definition of
\Phi^{-1}
, for any
\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\mu}^{\prime}\in\Phi^{-1}(Q_{a,b}^{\prime})
, we have
\|\tilde{\mu}-\tilde{\mu}^{\prime}\|_{2}\geq\varepsilon.
We have thus lower bounded the metric entropy of
Q_{a,b}
, i.e., provided
\frac{\varepsilon}{b-a}\gtrsim\sqrt{n/n^{1/p}}
, we have

\log\mathcal{M}\left(\varepsilon,Q_{a,b}\right)\gtrsim\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{2\sqrt{n}(b-a)}\right)^{-2(p-1)}.

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.6
.

We prove the
a=0
and
b=1
case since the argument is identical otherwise. From our global entropy result in (
3.3
), there exist constants
0<c_{1}<c_{2}
such that

\displaystyle c_{1}\cdot\left(\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2\sqrt{n}}\right)^{-2(p-1)}

\displaystyle\leq\log\mathcal{M}\left(\varepsilon,Q_{0,1}\right)\leq\log\mathcal{M}\left(\varepsilon/c^{\ast},Q_{0,1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}\cdot\left(\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2c^{\ast}\sqrt{n}}\right)^{-2(p-1)}.

Assume
c^{\ast}>((1+c_{1})/c_{2})^{\tfrac{1}{2(p-1)}}
. Then,

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{M}\left(\varepsilon/c^{\ast},Q_{0,1}\right)-\log\mathcal{M}\left(\varepsilon,Q_{0,1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq c_{2}\cdot\left(\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2c^{\ast}\sqrt{n}}\right)^{-2(p-1)}-c_{1}\cdot\left(\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2\sqrt{n}}\right)^{-2(p-1)}

\displaystyle=\left(\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2\sqrt{n}}\right)^{-2(p-1)}\left[c_{2}\cdot(c^{\ast})^{2(p-1)}-c_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\gtrsim\left(\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2\sqrt{n}}\right)^{-2(p-1)}.

Hence using (
3.1
),

\displaystyle\left(\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2\sqrt{n}}\right)^{-2(p-1)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log\mathcal{M}_{Q_{0,1}}^{\operatorname{loc}}\left(\varepsilon\right)\leq\log\mathcal{M}\left(\varepsilon/c^{\ast},Q_{0,1}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2\sqrt{n}}\right)^{-2(p-1)}.

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.7
.

Take
\varepsilon\asymp\sigma^{1/p}
, noting that this satisfies
\frac{1}{n^{1/2p}}\lesssim\varepsilon\lesssim 1
by our assumption on
\sigma
. Then we can verify that
\varepsilon^{2}/\sigma^{2}\asymp\sigma^{\tfrac{-2(p-1)}{p}}\asymp\log\mathcal{M}_{Q_{-1/\sqrt{n},1/\sqrt{n}}}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)
using (
3.4
). Note that the minimum with
1
is needed as if
\sigma^{1/p}
is larger than the diameter of
K
then the logarithm of local metric entropy becomes 0. Thus when
\varepsilon\asymp\sigma^{1/p}
, there exists absolute constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that
\varepsilon^{2}/\sigma^{2}\geq C_{1}\log\mathcal{M}_{Q_{-1/\sqrt{n},1/\sqrt{n}}}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)
and
\varepsilon^{2}/\sigma^{2}\leq C_{2}\log\mathcal{M}_{Q_{-1/\sqrt{n},1/\sqrt{n}}}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)
. Let us now show that this implies
\varepsilon^{\ast}\asymp\sigma^{1/p}
.

First, take
C>\max(1,\sqrt{C_{2}})
. Then by the non-increasing property of the metric entropy
[
Neykov, 2022
, Lemma II.8]
, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}/\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\log\mathcal{M}_{Q_{-1/\sqrt{n},1/\sqrt{n}}}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}\log\mathcal{M}_{Q_{-1/\sqrt{n},1/\sqrt{n}}}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon/C),

which rearranges to
(\varepsilon/C)^{2}/\sigma^{2}\leq\log\mathcal{M}_{Q_{-1/\sqrt{n},1/\sqrt{n}}}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon/C)
. By definition as a supremum, we have
\varepsilon^{\ast}\geq\varepsilon/C
.

Then take
0<C^{\prime}<\min(1,\sqrt{C_{1}})
. Then by the non-increasing property of the metric entropy, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}/\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle\geq C_{1}\log\mathcal{M}_{Q_{-1/\sqrt{n},1/\sqrt{n}}}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\geq(C^{\prime})^{2}\log\mathcal{M}_{Q_{-1/\sqrt{n},1/\sqrt{n}}}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon/C^{\prime}),

which rearranges to
(\varepsilon/C^{\prime})^{2}/\sigma^{2}>\log\mathcal{M}_{Q_{-1/\sqrt{n},1/\sqrt{n}}}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon/C^{\prime})
. By definition, we have
\varepsilon^{\ast}\leq\varepsilon/C^{\prime}
. Thus,
\varepsilon^{\ast}\asymp\varepsilon\asymp\sigma^{1/p}
, and the minimax rate is given by
{\varepsilon^{\ast}}^{2}\asymp\sigma^{2/p}
.

We now upper bound
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}
. Observe that
Q_{-1/\sqrt{n},1/\sqrt{n}}\subseteq B_{2}(2)
. Set
K=Q_{-1/\sqrt{n},1/\sqrt{n}}=Q_{-1/\sqrt{n},1/\sqrt{n}}\cap B_{2}(2)
, and then using Remark
2.8
and
Han et al. [2019, Proposition 5]
(using
B_{2}(2)
in place of
B_{2}(1)
simply scales the result), for
\sigma=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\sigma w(K)}=\sqrt{\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot w(K)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot n^{1/2-1/p}\cdot\log^{4}(n)}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{1/p}\log^{2}n.

∎

Lemma D.1
.

Let
a>0
and consider
K=[-a,a]
. Then the estimator defined in
Neykov [2022]
is the projection
\Pi_{K}Y
, which is the constrained least squares estimator.

Proof.

First, observe that when
Y\in K
and
K
is bounded, the estimator
\nu^{\ast}
from
Neykov [2022]
will output
Y
. This can be seen from the proof of Lemma V.2 of
Neykov [2022]
, where it is shown
\nu^{\ast}
will be within distance
6(C+1)\|Y-\Pi_{K}Y\|_{2}
from the projection
\Pi_{K}Y
. But in this case,
\Pi_{K}Y=Y
, so
\nu^{\ast}=\Pi_{K}Y=Y
.

Suppose
Y\not\in[-a,a]
, and suppose without loss of generality that
Y>a
. Let
\nu_{0}
be an arbitrary point in
K
that starts the algorithm of
Neykov [2022]
and let
c
be the constant appearing in the local metric entropy. Let
\nu_{k}
denote the
k
th update. We claim for each
k
,
|\nu_{k}-a|\leq d/2^{k}
where
d=2a
is the diameter of
K
(ignoring some absolute constants). This will prove that
\nu_{k}\to a
. We proceed by induction. For
k=1
, observe that we form a
d/c
-packing of
B(\nu_{0},d)\cap K=K
, and the first update
\nu_{1}
is the point closest to
Y
, and therefore to
a
. Since we form a
(d/c)
-maximal packing of
K
, this also implies a
(d/c)
-covering of
K
by the packing set points, so we must have
|\nu_{1}-a|\leq d/c\leq d/2^{1}
, verifying the base case. Now suppose
|\nu_{k}-a|\leq d/2^{k}
holds for some
k\in\mathbb{N}
. To obtain
\nu_{k+1}
, we form a
d/(2^{k}c)
-packing of
B(\nu_{k},d/2^{k})\cap K
. Then
\nu_{k+1}
will be the point closest to
Y
, and therefore
a
. Because
|\nu_{k}-a|\leq d/2^{k}
, we know
a\in B(\nu_{k},d/2^{k})\cap K
. Thus,
a
is within distance
d/(2^{k}c)\leq d/2^{k+1}
of one of the points in the maximal packing set of
B(\nu_{k},d/2^{k})\cap K
, in particular
\nu_{k+1}
. This completes the induction.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.8
.

First, we show that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}\wedge\sigma^{2}\lesssim(k+1)\sigma^{2}\wedge\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}
. It is trivial that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}\wedge\sigma^{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}
, and using the definition of
k
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}\wedge\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n-k-1}a_{i}^{2}\wedge\sigma^{2}+\sum_{i=n-k}^{n}a_{i}^{2}\wedge\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n-k-1}a_{i}^{2}+\sum_{i=n-k}^{n}\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n-k-1}a_{i}^{2}+(k+1)\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2(k+2)\sigma^{2}.

Thus
\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}\wedge\sigma^{2}\lesssim(k+1)\sigma^{2}\wedge\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}
.

Now we show
\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}\wedge\sigma^{2}\geq(k+1)\sigma^{2}\wedge\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}
. We consider two cases. First, suppose
a_{n-k}^{2}<\sigma^{2}
. Then by ordering of the
a_{i}
and definition of
k
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}\wedge\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{n-k}a_{i}^{2}\wedge\sigma^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-k}a_{i}^{2}\geq(k+1)\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle\geq(k+1)\sigma^{2}\wedge\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}.

On the other hand, if
a_{n-k}\geq\sigma^{2}
then

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}\wedge\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=n-k}^{n}a_{i}^{2}\wedge\sigma^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-k}\sigma^{2}=(k+1)\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle\geq(k+1)\sigma^{2}\wedge\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}.

Thus, we have the desired lower bound on
\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}\wedge\sigma^{2}
in both cases.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.9
.

It is known
[Section III.D
Neykov, 2022
, e.g.,]
that the global entropy satisfies

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{M}(\varepsilon,K)\asymp\begin{cases}\frac{\log(\varepsilon^{2}n)}{\varepsilon^{2}}&\varepsilon\gtrsim 1/\sqrt{n}\\
n&\varepsilon\asymp 1/\sqrt{n}\\
n\log\frac{1}{n\varepsilon^{2}}&\varepsilon\lesssim 1/\sqrt{n}.\end{cases}

Recall also the earlier result (
3.1
) which is due to
Yang and Barron [1999]
. In the
\varepsilon\gtrsim 1/\sqrt{n}
case, we assume
\varepsilon\geq{c^{\ast}}^{2}/\sqrt{n}
, so that
\log(\varepsilon^{2}n)/2\log({c^{\ast}}^{2})\geq 1
.
From the global entropy result stated above, since both
\varepsilon
and
\varepsilon/c^{\ast}
are
\gtrsim 1/\sqrt{n}
, there exist absolute constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{M}(\varepsilon,K)

\displaystyle\leq\log\mathcal{M}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast},K)\leq c_{1}\cdot\frac{\log(\varepsilon^{2}n)}{\varepsilon^{2}},

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{M}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast},K)

\displaystyle\geq c_{2}\cdot\frac{\log(\varepsilon^{2}n/{c^{\ast}}^{2})}{\varepsilon^{2}/{c^{\ast}}^{2}}.

Suppose
c^{\ast}
is taken sufficiently large such that
c_{2}{c^{\ast}}^{2}/2>c_{1}
. Hence

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{M}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast},K)-\log\mathcal{M}(\varepsilon,K)

\displaystyle\geq c_{2}\cdot\frac{\log(\varepsilon^{2}n/{c^{\ast}}^{2})}{\varepsilon^{2}/{c^{\ast}}^{2}}-c_{1}\cdot\frac{\log(\varepsilon^{2}n)}{\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle=(c_{2}{c^{\ast}}^{2}-c_{1})\cdot\frac{\log(\varepsilon^{2}n)}{\varepsilon^{2}}-c_{2}{c^{\ast}}^{2}\cdot\frac{\log({c^{\ast}}^{2})}{\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq(c_{2}{c^{\ast}}^{2}-c_{1})\cdot\frac{\log(\varepsilon^{2}n)}{\varepsilon^{2}}-\frac{c_{2}{c^{\ast}}^{2}\log({c^{\ast}}^{2})}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot\frac{\log(\varepsilon^{2}n)}{2\log({c^{\ast}}^{2})}

\displaystyle=(c_{2}{c^{\ast}}^{2}/2-c_{1})\cdot\frac{\log(\varepsilon^{2}n)}{\varepsilon^{2}}.

Thus, using (
3.1
),

\displaystyle(c_{2}{c^{\ast}}^{2}/2-c_{1})\cdot\frac{\log(\varepsilon^{2}n)}{\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\leq\log\mathcal{M}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast},K)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log(\varepsilon^{2}n)}{\varepsilon^{2}}.

Thus,
\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)\asymp\frac{\log(\varepsilon^{2}n)}{\varepsilon^{2}}
when
\varepsilon\gtrsim 1/\sqrt{n}
for a sufficiently large constant and with
c^{\ast}
chosen appropriately large.

Next, consider the case where
\varepsilon\leq{c^{\ast}}^{2}/\sqrt{n}
, i.e.,
\varepsilon\asymp 1/\sqrt{n}
or
\varepsilon\lesssim 1/\sqrt{n}
. case. Since
\eta\mapsto\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\eta)
is non-increasing, we know

\displaystyle n

\displaystyle\gtrsim\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)\geq\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}({c^{\ast}}^{2}/\sqrt{n})

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{\log(({c^{\ast}}^{2}/\sqrt{n})^{2}\cdot n)}{({c^{\ast}}^{2}/\sqrt{n})^{2}}\asymp n.

The first inequality was proven in Case 2 of the proof of Corollary
2.12
.
∎

D.2
Proofs for Section
3.2

Proof of Lemma
3.11
.

Consider the balls
B(0,\|v\|_{2}/2)
and
B(v,\|v\|_{2}/2)
. We have
w(B(0,\|v\|_{2}/2)\cap P)\geq w(K)
because
K\subseteq B(0,\|v\|_{2}/2)\cap P
. To see this, note clearly
K\subseteq P
and for any
k\in K
, using symmetry we have

\|k\|_{2}=\tfrac{1}{2}\|k-(-k)\|_{2}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\mathrm{diam}(K)\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\|v\|_{2}.

Next,
B(v,\|v\|_{2}/2)\cap P
is contained in the set
Q=\cup_{\beta\in[0,1]}[\beta v+(1-\beta)(v/2+K/2)]
. To see this, suppose
x\in B(v,\|v\|_{2}/2)\cap P
. Then
x=\alpha v+(1-\alpha)k
for some
k\in K
and
\alpha\in[0,1]
and must satisfy
\|x-v\|_{2}\leq\|v\|_{2}/2
. This implies

0\leq(1-\alpha)\|v-k\|_{2}=\|x-v\|_{2}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\|v\|_{2}.

Since
\|v-k\|_{2}=\sqrt{\|v\|_{2}^{2}+\|k\|_{2}^{2}}
by the Pythagorean theorem and orthogonality of
v
to
K
, we can show
1-\alpha\leq 1/2
, i.e.,
\alpha\geq 1/2
, since

(1-\alpha)^{2}\leq\frac{\|v\|_{2}^{2}}{4\|v-k\|^{2}}=\frac{1}{4}\cdot\frac{\|v\|_{2}^{2}}{\|v\|_{2}^{2}+\|k\|_{2}^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{4}.

Now take
\beta=2(\alpha-1/2)\in[0,1]
, and write

x=\left(\tfrac{1}{2}+\tfrac{\beta}{2}\right)v+\left(\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{\beta}{2}\right)k=\beta v+(1-\beta)\left(\tfrac{v}{2}+\tfrac{k}{2}\right)\in Q.

Therefore
w(Q)\geq w(B(v,\|v\|_{2}/2)\cap P)
.

For any
a\in\mathbb{R}
, define
a_{+}=\max(0,a).
Then we compute

\displaystyle w(Q)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\sup_{q\in Q}\langle\xi,q\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\sup_{\beta\in[0,1],k\in K}\langle\xi,\beta v+(1-\beta)(v/2+k/2)\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\sup_{\beta\in[0,1]}\beta\langle\xi,v/2\rangle+\langle\xi,v/2\rangle+(1-\beta)\sup_{k\in K}\langle\xi,k/2\rangle]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\sup_{\beta\in[0,1]}\beta\langle\xi,v/2\rangle+(1-\beta)\sup_{k\in K}\langle\xi,k/2\rangle]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\sup_{k\in K}\langle\xi,k/2\rangle]+\mathbb{E}[\sup_{\beta\in[0,1]}\beta\langle\xi,v/2\rangle-\beta\sup_{k\in K}\langle\xi,k/2\rangle]

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}w(K)+\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\xi,v/2\rangle-\sup_{k\in K}\langle\xi,k/2\rangle\right]_{+}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}w(K)+\mathbb{E}(\langle\xi,v/2\rangle)_{+}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}w(K)+\sqrt{2/\pi}\cdot\|v\|_{2}/4,

where in the last few lines, we evaluated the supremum at
\beta\in\{0,1\}
and used that
\sup_{k\in K}\langle\xi,k/2\rangle\geq 0
since
0\in K
. The final line used properties of the rectified Gaussian distribution, proved in, e.g.,
Beauchamp [2018, Appendix A]
. Recall we assume
\|v\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim w(K)
. We thus have

\displaystyle w_{P,v}(\|v\|_{2}/2)

\displaystyle=w(B(v,\|v\|_{2}/2)\cap P)\leq w(Q)

\displaystyle\lesssim\tfrac{1}{2}w(K)+\|v\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tfrac{1}{2}w(K)+\sqrt{w(K)}.

Hence

\displaystyle w_{P,0}(\|v\|_{2}/2)-w_{P,v}(\|v\|_{2}/2)

\displaystyle\geq w(K)-w_{P,v}(\|v\|_{2}/2)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{1}{2}w(K)-\sqrt{w(K)}.

So if
w(K)
is sufficiently big, then
w_{P,0}(\|v\|_{2}/2)-w_{P,v}(\|v\|_{2}/2)\gtrsim w(K)
.

Hence we have for constants
\kappa,\tilde{C}
that

\displaystyle w_{P,0}(\|v\|_{2}/2)-w_{P,v}(\|v\|_{2}/2)\geq\kappa w(K)\geq\tilde{C}\|v\|_{2}^{2}.

Then recalling the definition of
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)
in Theorem
2.15
(take
\varepsilon=c^{\ast}\|v\|_{2}/2
,
\nu_{1}=v
,
\nu_{2}=0
), we obtain
\overline{\varepsilon}\gtrsim\|v\|_{2}
. This implies for sufficiently large
c
(so that
\overline{\varepsilon}\gtrsim 1=\sigma
) that
\varepsilon_{P,\mathrm{LS}}\gtrsim\overline{\varepsilon}\gtrsim\|v\|_{2}
.
∎

Lemma D.2
.

Set
K=Q_{-1/\sqrt{n},1/\sqrt{n}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
where
n^{1/p}\in\mathbb{N}
for simplicity. Then for
0<t<1
,
w_{K,0}(t)\gtrsim tn^{1/2-1/p}
.

Proof of Lemma
D.2
.

The proof will be very similar to that of
Han et al. [2019, Proposition 5]
with some rescaling and the use of
Q_{a,b}
instead of their more general cone. By definition
w_{K,0}(t)=\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbb{I}_{n})}\sup_{q\in Q_{a,b}\cap B(0,t)}\langle\xi,q\rangle
where
a=-b=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
. As in
Han et al. [2019]
, define
W=\{l\in\mathbb{L}_{p,n}:\sum_{j=1}^{p}[l]_{j}=1\}
,
W^{+}=\{l\in\mathbb{L}_{p,n}:\sum_{j=1}^{p}[l]_{j}>1\}
, and
W^{-}=\{l\in\mathbb{L}_{p,n}:\sum_{j=1}^{p}[l]_{j}<1\}
, where
[l]_{j}
is the
j
th component of
l\in\mathbb{L}_{p,n}\subset\mathbb{R}^{p}
. For any draw of
\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbb{I}_{n})
, where coordinate
i
corresponds to
l_{i}\in\mathbb{L}_{p,n}
, define
\Theta(\xi)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
as

[\Theta(\xi)]_{i}=\begin{cases}1/\sqrt{n}&\text{if }l_{i}\in W^{+}\\
\mathrm{sgn}(\xi_{i})/\sqrt{n}&\text{if }l_{i}\in W\\
-1/\sqrt{n}&\text{if }l_{i}\in W^{-}.\end{cases}

Observe that
\|\Theta(\xi)\|_{2}=1
, and moreover
\Theta(\xi)\in Q_{a,b}
. Thus, since
0<t<1
, we have
t\Theta(\xi)\in Q_{a,b}\cap B(0,t)
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbb{I}_{n})}\sup_{q\in Q_{a,b}\cap B(0,t)}\langle\xi,q\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbb{I}_{n})}\langle\xi,t\Theta(\xi)\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{t}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbb{I}_{n})}\left[\sum_{l_{i}\in W^{+}}\xi_{i}-\sum_{l_{i}\in W^{-}}\xi_{i}+\sum_{l_{i}\in W}|\xi_{i}|\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{t\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{\pi n}}|W|.

As stated in
Han et al. [2019]
,
|W|\geq\left(\frac{n^{1/p}-1}{p-1}\right)^{p-1}
, so we obtain
w_{K,0}(t)\gtrsim\frac{tn^{1/2-1/p}}{(p-1)^{p-1}}.

∎

Lemma D.3
.

Set
K=Q_{-1/\sqrt{n},1/\sqrt{n}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then for
0<c,t<1
, we have
w_{K,0}(ct)\lesssim ctn^{1/2-1/p}\log^{4}(n)
.

Proof of Lemma
D.3
.

In Proposition 5 of
Han et al. [2019]
, the authors consider a cone which they denote
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{L}_{p,n})
—to avoid clashing notations, we write it as
\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{L}_{p,n})
. Observe that

\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{L}_{p,n})\cap B(0,ct)=ct\cdot\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{L}_{p,n})\cap B(0,1).

Thus,
w(\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{L}_{p,n})\cap B(0,ct))=ct\cdot w(\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{L}_{p,n})\cap B(0,1))
. By
Han et al. [2019, Proposition 5]
, we have

w(\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{L}_{p,n}))\cap B(0,ct))\lesssim ctn^{1/2-1/p}\log^{4}n.

But
K\cap B(0,ct)
is a subset of
\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{L}_{p,n})\cap B(0,ct),
so

\displaystyle w_{K,0}(ct)\lesssim ctn^{1/2-1/p}\log^{4}n.

∎

Proof of Lemma
3.13
.

We define 4 sets and relate their Gaussian widths. Let
T_{1}=B(0,b/4)\cap K
and
T_{2}=\bigcup_{x\in[0,b/4]}(\{x\}\times B_{x})
. Let
B^{n}(s,r)
mean the
n
-dimensional ball centered at
s
and with radius
r
. Then let
T_{3}=B^{n}(b/2e_{1},f(b/2))\cap K
and
T_{4}=B^{n}(b/2e_{1},b/4)\cap K
where
e_{1}=(1,0,\dots,0)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

First, observe that
T_{1}\subseteq T_{2}
. To see this, note that if
u\in T_{1}
, then
u\in K
so that
u\in\{x\}\times B_{x}
for some
x\in[0,b]
. This means the first coordinate of
u
is
x
. Hence
x\leq\|u\|_{2}\leq b/4
using that
u\in B(0,b/4)
, so indeed
u\in T_{2}
. Hence
w(T_{1})\leq w(T_{2})
.

Now let us compute
w(T_{2})
. As
T_{2}
is convex, for a fixed Gaussian vector
g
, the solution to
\arg\sup_{x\in T_{2}}\langle x,g\rangle
will lie on an extreme point of
T_{2}
, i.e.,
x=(c\cdot b/4)\cdot e_{1}+f(c\cdot b/4)\cdot u
for some
c\in[0,1]
and unit vector
u\in e_{1}^{\perp}
. Thus, letting
y_{+}:=\max(0,y)
, we have

\displaystyle w(T_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{g}\sup_{x\in T_{2}}\langle x,g\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{g}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\in[0,1]\\
u\in e_{1}^{\perp},\|u\|_{2}=1\end{subarray}}\langle(c\cdot b/4)\cdot e_{1}+f(c\cdot b/4)\cdot u,g\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{g}\sup_{u\in e_{1}^{\perp},\|u\|_{2}=1}[(b/4)\langle e_{1},g\rangle_{+}+f(b/4)\langle u,g\rangle_{+}]

\displaystyle\leq(b/4)\cdot\mathbb{E}_{g}\langle e_{1},g\rangle_{+}+f(b/4)\cdot\mathbb{E}_{g}\sup_{u\in e_{1}^{\perp},\|u\|_{2}=1}\langle u,g\rangle_{+}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{b}{4\sqrt{2\pi}}+f(b/4)\sqrt{n-1}

\displaystyle\leq f(b/2)\sqrt{n-1}.

In the fourth line, we note that
\sup_{u\in e_{1}^{\perp},\|u\|_{2}=1}\langle u,g\rangle_{+}
is achieved with
u=(0,g_{-1}/\|g_{-1}\|)
, where
g_{-1}
is the random vector in
\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
obtained from removing the first coordinate of
g
. Hence
\mathbb{E}_{g}\sup_{u\in e_{1}^{\perp},\|u\|_{2}=1}\langle u,g\rangle_{+}=\mathbb{E}_{g}\|g_{-1}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{n-1}
. In the same line, we also used that
\mathbb{E}_{g}\langle e_{1},g\rangle_{+}=\mathbb{E}\max(0,g_{1})=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}

[see
Beauchamp, 2018
, Appendix A]
. The final inequality was from our assumption (
3.7
) about the secant line.

Proceeding to
T_{3}
, note this set has width at least
\sqrt{n-1}\cdot f(b/2)
up to constants by
Vershynin [2018, Example 7.5.7]
since it contains a
(n-1)
-dimensional ball of radius
f(b/2)
. Note that
T_{3}\subset T_{4}
since
b/4>f(b/2)
. Putting our results together, we obtain

\displaystyle w_{K,0}(b/4)

\displaystyle=w(T_{1})\leq w(T_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{b}{4\sqrt{2\pi}}+f(b/4)\sqrt{n-1}

\displaystyle\leq f(b/2)\sqrt{n-1}

\displaystyle\lesssim w(T_{3})\leq w(T_{4})=w_{K,b/2\cdot e_{1}}(b/4).

This means

\displaystyle w_{K,b/2\cdot e_{1}}(b/4)-w_{K,0}(b/4)

\displaystyle\geq f(b/2)\sqrt{n-1}-\left(\frac{b}{4\sqrt{2\pi}}+f(b/4)\sqrt{n-1}\right).

For sufficiently large
n
and any constant
\overline{C}>0
, we have

f(b/2)\cdot\sqrt{n-1}-\frac{b}{4\sqrt{2\pi}}-f(b/4)\cdot\sqrt{n-1}\geq\overline{C}b^{2},

e.g., take
n\geq 1+\left(\frac{\overline{C}b^{2}+b/(4\sqrt{2\pi})}{f(b/2)-f(b/4)}\right)^{2}
. This implies
w_{K,b/2\cdot e_{1}}(b/4)-w_{K,0}(b/4)\geq\overline{C}b^{2}
. Take
\sigma=1
. Now consider the inequality defining
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)
in Theorem
2.18
. Taking
\nu_{1}=(b/2)\cdot e_{1}
,
\nu_{2}=0
,
\varepsilon=bc^{\ast}/4
, and choosing
\overline{C}
sufficiently large, we can show that
\varepsilon
satisfies the condition in the definition of
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)
, hence
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\geq\varepsilon\asymp b
. Noting that
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\gtrsim b\gtrsim\sigma
, we conclude
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\gtrsim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\gtrsim b
by the theorem.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.14
.

We first show that if
\sigma\gtrsim d^{2}/w(K)
, then for all
y\in K
, we have
w_{y}(\varepsilon_{y,w})\gtrsim w(K)
. By Jung’s theorem, setting
\kappa_{n}=\sqrt{\frac{n}{2(n+1)}}\asymp 1
, for some
\mu\in K
we have
K\subset B(\mu,d\kappa_{n})
. Using our Lipschitz result
4
4

4

This can be rearranged to the form
w_{\nu}(\varepsilon)\geq\frac{w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)}{1+\|\mu-\nu\|/\varepsilon}
.
in (
2.4
) and that
\|y-\mu\|\leq d\kappa_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle w_{y}(d\kappa_{n})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{w_{\mu}(d\kappa_{n})}{1+\tfrac{\|y-\mu\|}{d\kappa_{n}}}\geq\frac{w_{\mu}(d\kappa_{n})}{2}=\frac{w(K)}{2}.

Then by definition of
\varepsilon_{y,w}
,

\displaystyle w_{y}(\varepsilon_{y,w})-\frac{\varepsilon_{y,w}^{2}}{2\sigma}

\displaystyle\geq w_{y}(d\kappa_{n})-\frac{(d\kappa_{n})^{2}}{2\sigma}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{w(K)}{2}-\frac{(d\kappa_{n})^{2}}{2\sigma}.

Hence, for
\sigma\gtrsim d^{2}/w(K)
we have
w_{y}(\varepsilon_{y,w})\gtrsim w(K)
.

Next, recall we chose
x\in\operatorname{bd}K
that minimizes
\|\nabla G(x)\|_{2}
and set
x^{\ast}=\frac{\nabla G(x)}{\|\nabla G(x)\|_{2}}
. We now upper bound
w_{x}(\varepsilon_{x,w})
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{y\in K\cap B(x,\varepsilon_{x,w})}\langle\xi,y\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\sup_{y\in K\cap B(x,\varepsilon_{x,w})}\langle\xi,y-x\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\sup_{z\in K^{\prime}}\langle\xi,z\rangle

where
K^{\prime}=\{z:z+x\in K,\|z\|\leq\varepsilon_{x,w}\}
. Now write
\xi=\sum_{i}\alpha_{i}\mu_{i}
and
z=\sum_{i}\alpha_{i}^{\prime}\mu_{i}
where
\mu_{i}
are the ortho-normalized eigenvectors of
\tilde{M}/2
. Using (
3.10
), observe that that
K^{\prime}
is a subset of
K^{\prime\prime}=\{z:\|z\|\leq\varepsilon_{x,w},-\langle x^{\ast},z\rangle\geq z^{T}\tilde{M}z/2\}
. Thus, indexing
K^{\prime\prime}
by
\alpha^{\prime}
in the supremum and using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle w_{x}(\varepsilon_{x,w})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\sup_{z\in K^{\prime}}\langle\xi,z\rangle\leq\mathbb{E}\sup_{z\in K^{\prime\prime}}\langle\xi,z\rangle=\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\sup_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sum_{i}\alpha_{i}\alpha_{i}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\sup_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sqrt{\sum\alpha_{i}^{\prime 2}\lambda_{i}}\sqrt{\sum\alpha_{i}^{2}/\lambda_{i}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sqrt{\sum\alpha_{i}^{\prime 2}\lambda_{i}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\sum\alpha_{i}^{2}/\lambda_{i}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sqrt{\sum\alpha_{i}^{\prime 2}\lambda_{i}}\sqrt{\sum 1/\lambda_{i}}.

The final two lines used Jensen’s inequality and then that
\mathbb{E}_{\xi}\alpha_{i}^{2}=1
as the
\alpha_{i}
are standard Gaussian random variables.

On the other hand
-\langle x^{*},z\rangle\geq z{{}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}\tilde{M}z/2
is equivalent to
\sum\alpha_{i}^{\prime 2}\lambda_{i}\leq-\sum\alpha_{i}^{\prime}\langle\mu_{i},x^{*}\rangle
, while
\|z\|\leq\varepsilon_{x,w}
is equivalent to
\sum\alpha_{i}^{\prime 2}\leq\varepsilon_{x,w}^{2}
. Thus by another application of Cauchy-Schwarz we get

\displaystyle w_{x}(\varepsilon_{x,w})

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sqrt{-\sum\alpha_{i}^{\prime}\langle\mu_{i},x^{*}\rangle}\sqrt{\sum 1/\lambda_{i}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sqrt{\sum\alpha_{i}^{\prime 2}}\sqrt{\sum\langle\mu_{i},x^{*}\rangle^{2}}}\sqrt{\sum 1/\lambda_{i}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon_{x,w}}\sqrt{\sum 1/\lambda_{i}}.

This shows
w(K)\lesssim w_{x}(\varepsilon_{x,w})\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon_{x,w}}\sqrt{\sum 1/\lambda_{i}}
provided
\sigma\gtrsim d^{2}/(w(K))
, and therefore that
\varepsilon_{x,w}\gtrsim w^{2}(K)/\sum(1/\lambda_{i})
. So taking the supremum over
x\in K
, we have
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\gtrsim w^{2}(K)/\sum(1/\lambda_{i})
for
\sigma\gtrsim d^{2}/(w(K))
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.15
.

In Remark
2.17
, we found for each
\mu,\nu\in K
that
|w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)-w_{\nu}(\varepsilon)|\leq\|\mu-\nu\|\cdot\frac{w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)\wedge w_{\nu}(\varepsilon)}{\varepsilon}
. Specializing to
\mu=0
, we obtain
w_{\nu}(\varepsilon)\geq w_{0}(\varepsilon)/(\|\nu\|/\varepsilon+1)
. Now pick
\nu=(0,\ldots,1/d_{n-k},0,\ldots,0)
, where all but coordinate
n-k
is zero. Set
\delta=1/d_{n-k}
so that
\|\nu\|/\delta=1
, and set
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{\nu,w}
as defined in (
2.1
). Then we have
w_{\nu}(\delta)\geq w_{0}(\delta)/2.

Hence by taking
\sigma\gtrsim\frac{\delta^{2}}{w_{0}(\delta)}
we obtain

\displaystyle w_{\nu}(\varepsilon_{\nu,w})-\frac{\varepsilon_{\nu,w}^{2}}{2\sigma}

\displaystyle\geq w_{\nu}(\delta)-\frac{\delta^{2}}{2\sigma}\geq\frac{w_{0}(\delta)}{2}-\frac{\delta^{2}}{2\sigma}\gtrsim w_{0}(\delta).

The first inequality used the definition of
\varepsilon_{\nu,w}
. For such
\sigma
, we have

\displaystyle w_{\nu}(\varepsilon_{\nu,w})\gtrsim w_{0}(\delta).

(D.1)

Now we will attempt to control
w_{\nu}(\varepsilon_{\nu,w})
from above following a similar logic to before. We need to calculate
\mathbb{E}\sup_{y\in K^{\prime}}\langle\xi,y\rangle=\mathbb{E}\sup_{y\in K^{\prime}}\langle\xi,y-\nu\rangle
for
K^{\prime}=\{y\in K:\|\nu-y\|\leq\varepsilon_{\nu,w}\}
. Note that
\nu
is a boundary point of
K
since
\|D\nu\|_{2}^{2}=1
. Set
x^{*}=\frac{\nabla G(\nu)}{\|\nabla G(\nu)\|}
with
G(\nu)=\|D\nu\|_{2}^{2}
,
M=2D^{2}
,
\tilde{M}=M/\|\nabla G(\nu)\|
. Also,
\nabla G(\nu)=2D^{2}\nu=(0,0,\ldots,2d_{n-k},0,\ldots,0)
,
x^{*}=(0,0,\ldots,1,0,0,\ldots,0)
, and
\tilde{M}/2
is the diagonal matrix with element
d_{i}^{2}/(2d_{n-k})
in entry
(i,i)
. Using (
3.8
),
K^{\prime}
is a subset of

B(\nu,\varepsilon_{\nu,w})\cap\Big\{y\in K:\langle x^{*},\nu-y\rangle\geq\frac{(\nu-y){{}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}\tilde{M}(\nu-y)}{2}\Big\}.

Let
y-\nu=z
. We need to optimize

\langle\xi,z\rangle
given that
\|z\|\leq\varepsilon_{\nu,w}
,
-\langle x^{*},z\rangle\geq z{{}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}\tilde{M}z/2
. We have by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{z}\langle\xi,z\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\sup_{z}\left[\sum_{i=n-k}^{n}\xi_{i}z_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-k-1}\xi_{i}z_{i}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{\nu,w}\sqrt{k+1}+\mathbb{E}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n-k-1}\!\frac{\xi_{i}^{2}}{\lambda_{i}}}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n-k-1}\!\!\!z_{i}^{2}\lambda_{i}},

where the
\lambda_{i}=d_{i}^{2}/(2d_{n-k})
are the eigenvalues of
\tilde{M}/2
. Bringing the expectation inside the square root by Jensen’s inequality and noting that
\mathbb{E}\xi_{i}^{2}=1
we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{z}\langle\xi,z\rangle\leq\varepsilon_{\nu,w}\sqrt{k+1}+\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n-k-1}\!\frac{2d_{n-k}}{d_{i}^{2}}}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n-k-1}\!\!z_{i}^{2}\lambda_{i}}.

Observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n-k-1}z_{i}^{2}\lambda_{i}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}z_{i}^{2}\lambda_{i}=z^{T}(\tilde{M}/2)z

\displaystyle\leq-2\langle x^{*},z\rangle\leq 2\varepsilon_{\nu,w}.

Recalling (
D.1
) (which requires
\sigma\gtrsim\delta^{2}/w_{0}(\delta)
) and combining the previous two lines,

\displaystyle w_{0}(\delta)

\displaystyle\lesssim w_{\nu}(\varepsilon_{\nu,w})\leq\mathbb{E}\sup_{z}\langle\xi,z\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{\nu,w}\sqrt{k+1}+\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n-k-1}\frac{2d_{n-k}}{d_{i}^{2}}}\cdot\sqrt{2\varepsilon_{\nu,w}}.

(D.2)

Now we note several facts. The set
B(0,\delta)\cap K
is all
x
such that
\|Dx\|_{2}\leq 1
, and
\|x\|\leq\delta
. Let
D^{\prime}
be the diagonal matrix with
(i,i)
entry
d_{i}^{\prime}
, where where
d_{i}^{\prime}=d_{i}
for
i\leq n-k-1
and
d_{i}^{\prime}=1/\delta
for
i\geq n-k
. If
\|D^{\prime}x\|\leq 1
then
\|Dx\|_{2}\leq 1
. Moreover,
\|D^{\prime}x\|\leq 1
implies
\|x\|\leq\delta
. Thus using
Wainwright [2019, Exercise 5.9]
to compute the Gaussian width, we obtain

\displaystyle w_{0}(\delta)

\displaystyle=w(B(0,\delta)\cap K)\geq w(\{x:\|D^{\prime}x\|\leq 1\})

\displaystyle\gtrsim\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{d_{i}^{\prime 2}}}=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n-k-1}\frac{1}{d_{i}^{2}}+\sum_{i=n-k}^{n}\delta^{2}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\delta\sqrt{k+1}+\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n-k-1}\frac{1}{d_{i}^{2}}},

(D.3)

where in the last step we used that
\sqrt{a+b}\geq(\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b})/\sqrt{2}
for
a,b\geq 0
.

Combining this result with our upper bound on
w_{0}(\delta)
from (
D.2
), we conclude

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\nu,w}\sqrt{k+1}+\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n-k-1}\frac{4\varepsilon_{\nu,w}d_{n-k}}{d_{i}^{2}}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\delta\sqrt{k+1}+\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n-k-1}\frac{1}{d_{i}^{2}}}.

From the above we have that either
\varepsilon_{\nu,w}\sqrt{k+1}\gtrsim\delta\sqrt{k+1}
or
\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n-k-1}\frac{4\varepsilon_{\nu,w}d_{n-k}}{d_{i}^{2}}}\gtrsim\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{n-k-1}\frac{1}{d_{i}^{2}}}
; in the first case, we conclude that
\varepsilon_{\nu,w}\gtrsim\delta
; in the second case we obtain
\varepsilon_{\nu,w}\gtrsim 1/d_{n-k}=\delta
. Hence
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\geq\varepsilon_{\nu,w}(\sigma)\gtrsim\delta_{n-k}
for
\sigma\gtrsim\delta_{n-k}^{2}/w_{0}(\delta_{n-k})
, as claimed.

Next, suppose the LSE is minimal optimal for
K
for all
\sigma
. Take
\sigma\asymp\delta^{2}/w_{0}(\delta)
using the same
\delta=\delta_{n-k}
. First, suppose
\delta\gtrsim\sigma
for a sufficiently large constant, which means
\varepsilon_{\nu,w}\gtrsim\delta\gtrsim\sigma
using our previous result, where
\nu
is as chosen above. By Lemma
2.1
, we have
\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\nu\|_{2}^{2}\gtrsim\varepsilon_{\nu,w}^{2}
.

Using (
1.2
), observe that the minimax rate
\varepsilon^{\ast}
for this value of
\sigma
satisfies

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{*2}\delta^{-4}w^{2}_{0}(\delta)\asymp\varepsilon^{*2}/\sigma^{2}\lesssim\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon^{*}).

Furthermore, if the LSE is minimax optimal, we have
{\varepsilon^{*}}^{2}\gtrsim\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\nu\|_{2}^{2}\gtrsim\varepsilon_{\nu,w}^{2}\gtrsim\delta^{2}
. Hence that
\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon^{*})\leq\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(c\delta)
for a sufficiently small absolute constant
c
since
\varepsilon\mapsto\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)
is non-increasing. And also
\varepsilon^{*2}\delta^{-4}w^{2}_{0}(\delta)\gtrsim\delta^{-2}w^{2}_{0}(\delta)
. Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\delta^{-1}w_{0}(\delta)\lesssim\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(c\delta)}.

We now assume
\delta\lesssim\sigma
, i.e.,
\delta\lesssim\delta^{2}/w_{0}(\delta)
for a sufficiently large absolute constant. Rearranging,
w_{0}(\delta)/\delta\lesssim 1\leq\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(c\delta)}
since
\delta\leq d
for some
c<1
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.16
.

Note that if
\sigma\gtrsim d
, then by Lemma
1.4
the minimax rate satisfies
\varepsilon^{\ast}\gtrsim d
which implies the LSE satisfies
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\gtrsim d
. Now consider the case where
\sigma\lesssim d
.

We fix
c>4
,
\kappa\in(1/c,1-2/c)
, and pick any
\varepsilon
such that
d\lesssim\varepsilon<\tfrac{d}{2}\wedge\tfrac{d}{2(\kappa+1/c)}
. Take a point
\mu\in K
which is a midpoint of a diameter. Pick
\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}\in K
such that
\|\mu-\nu^{\prime}\|=\|\mu-\nu^{\prime\prime}\|=\kappa\varepsilon<d/2
and
\mu=\frac{\nu^{\prime}+\nu^{\prime\prime}}{2}
. Note that
\|\nu^{\prime}-\nu^{\prime\prime}\|=2\kappa\varepsilon
. Define for some fixed
\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbb{I}_{n})
the points

\displaystyle u^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{x\in B(\nu^{\prime},\varepsilon/c)\cap K}\langle\xi,x\rangle,\quad u^{\prime\prime}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{x\in B(\nu^{\prime\prime},\varepsilon/c)\cap K}\langle\xi,x\rangle

in
K
. Apply strong convexity to
u^{\prime},u^{\prime\prime}
to conclude
B((u^{\prime}+u^{\prime\prime})/2,k\|u^{\prime}-u^{\prime\prime}\|_{2}^{2}/4)\subset K
. Then we repeatedly use
u^{\prime}\in B(\nu^{\prime},\varepsilon/c)
and
u^{\prime\prime}\in B(\nu^{\prime\prime},\varepsilon/c)
along with the triangle inequality to obtain

\displaystyle 2\kappa\varepsilon+2\varepsilon/c

\displaystyle\geq\overbrace{\|\nu^{\prime}-\nu^{\prime\prime}\|}^{=2\kappa\varepsilon}+\overbrace{\|u^{\prime}-\nu^{\prime}\|}^{\leq\varepsilon/c}+\overbrace{\|u^{\prime\prime}-\nu^{\prime\prime}\|}^{\leq\varepsilon/c}

(D.4)

\displaystyle\geq\|\nu^{\prime}-\nu^{\prime\prime}+u^{\prime}-\nu^{\prime}+\nu^{\prime\prime}-u^{\prime\prime}\|

\displaystyle=\|u^{\prime}-u^{\prime\prime}\|

\displaystyle\geq\|\nu^{\prime}-\nu^{\prime\prime}\|-\|\nu^{\prime}-u^{\prime}\|-\|u^{\prime\prime}-\nu^{\prime\prime}\|

\displaystyle\geq 2\varepsilon\kappa-2\varepsilon/c.

(D.5)

Consider the point
u=(u^{\prime}+u^{\prime\prime})/2+(\xi/\|\xi\|)\cdot k\|u^{\prime}-u^{\prime\prime}\|^{2}_{2}/4
. Then

\displaystyle\|u-\mu\|

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\|u^{\prime}-\nu^{\prime}\|+\tfrac{1}{2}\|u^{\prime\prime}-\nu^{\prime\prime}\|+\tfrac{1}{4}k\|u^{\prime}-u^{\prime\prime}\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\cdot\tfrac{\varepsilon}{c}+\tfrac{1}{2}\cdot\tfrac{\varepsilon}{c}+k(\varepsilon\kappa+\tfrac{\varepsilon}{c})^{2}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\varepsilon}{c}+k(\varepsilon\kappa+\tfrac{\varepsilon}{c})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{\varepsilon}{c}+2d^{-1}(\varepsilon\kappa+\tfrac{\varepsilon}{c})^{2}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\varepsilon}{c}+(\varepsilon\kappa+\tfrac{\varepsilon}{c})\cdot\tfrac{2\varepsilon(\kappa+1/c)}{d}

\displaystyle<\tfrac{\varepsilon}{c}+\varepsilon\kappa+\tfrac{\varepsilon}{c}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon(\kappa+\tfrac{2}{c})<1.

The first line used the triangle inequality and that
\mu=\tfrac{\nu^{\prime}+\nu^{\prime\prime}}{2}
. The second line used
u^{\prime}\in B(\nu^{\prime},\varepsilon/c)
and
u^{\prime\prime}\in B(\nu^{\prime\prime},\varepsilon/c)
along with our result from (
D.4
). The fourth line used
k<2d^{-1}
as we established in the main text. The final two lines used
\varepsilon<\frac{d}{2(\kappa+1/c)}
followed by
\kappa+2/c<1
. Hence
u\in B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K
.

Then using that
u\in B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K
along with (
D.5
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K}\langle\xi,x\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\langle\xi,u\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\xi,\tfrac{u^{\prime}+u^{\prime\prime}}{2}\rangle+\tfrac{1}{4}k\|\xi\|\|u^{\prime}-u^{\prime\prime}\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\langle\xi,u^{\prime}\rangle+\tfrac{1}{2}\langle\xi,u^{\prime\prime}\rangle+k\|\xi\|(\varepsilon\kappa-\varepsilon/c)^{2}.

Taking expectation with respect to
\xi
we have

\displaystyle w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\cdot w_{\nu^{\prime}}(\varepsilon/c)+\tfrac{1}{2}\cdot w_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}(\varepsilon/c)+\mathbb{E}\|\xi\|k(\varepsilon\kappa-\varepsilon/c)^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\inf_{x\in B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K}w_{x}(\varepsilon/c)+\sqrt{n}k\varepsilon^{2}(\kappa-1/c)^{2}.

The first line again used
u^{\prime}\in B(\nu^{\prime},\varepsilon/c)
and
u^{\prime\prime}\in B(\nu^{\prime\prime},\varepsilon/c)
, and the second that
\nu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}\in B(\mu,\kappa\varepsilon)\subset B(\mu,\varepsilon)
along with
\mathbb{E}\|\xi\|\gtrsim\sqrt{n}
(see
Chandrasekaran et al. [2012, Section 3.1]
).

Now assume
\sigma\asymp(k\sqrt{n})^{-1}
which implies
\sigma\gtrsim d/\sqrt{n}
since
k<4d^{-1}
. This implies

w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)\gtrsim\inf_{x\in B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K}w_{x}(\varepsilon/c)+\overline{C}\varepsilon^{2}/2\sigma

for some constant
\overline{C}>0
. Then by definition of
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)
in Theorem
2.14
we conclude
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\gtrsim\varepsilon\asymp d
. As we have
d\gtrsim\sigma
, we have
\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
which by Theorem
2.14
implies
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\gtrsim\overline{\varepsilon}(\sigma)\gtrsim d
. Then note that
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\leq d
always holds to deduce our claim.
∎

Appendix E
Proofs for Appendix
A

Lemma E.1
.

Let
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)=\sup_{\mu\in K}\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)
where
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}
is as defined in (
2.1
). Set
c^{\prime}=\frac{(2c^{\ast}-4)(4c^{\ast}-1/c^{\ast})}{(c^{\ast}-4)c^{\ast}}
for some
c^{\ast}>4
.

(i)

If Algorithm
2
terminates after
k
iterations of the
while
loop, it returns
\varepsilon=\frac{d}{2^{k-1}c^{\ast}}
and satisfies
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\geq\varepsilon
.

(ii)

If Algorithm
2
does not terminate within
k
iterations, then we have
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\leq c^{\prime}\cdot\frac{d}{2^{k}}
.

Proof of Lemma
E.1
.

Suppose that Algorithm
2
has terminated on the
k
th level of the infinite tree, i.e., has returned
d/(2^{k-1}c^{\ast})
. Then for some
\mu,\nu\in K
such that
\|\mu-\nu\|\leq d/2^{k-1}
, we have

\displaystyle w_{\mu}\left(\frac{d}{2^{k-1}}\right)-w_{\nu}\left(\frac{d}{2^{k-1}c^{\ast}}\right)\geq\frac{C(\tfrac{d}{2^{k-1}})^{2}}{2\sigma}.

Since
B(\mu,d/2^{k-1})\subseteq B(\nu,d/2^{k-2})
,

\displaystyle w_{\nu}\left(\frac{d}{2^{k-2}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq w_{\mu}\left(\frac{d}{2^{k-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq w_{\nu}\left(\frac{d}{2^{k-1}c^{\ast}}\right)+\frac{C(\tfrac{d}{2^{k-1}})^{2}}{2\sigma}.

Then since
C=4-\frac{1}{{c^{\ast}}^{2}}
, rearranging yields

w_{\nu}\left(\frac{d}{2^{k-2}}\right)-\frac{(\tfrac{d}{2^{k-2}})^{2}}{2\sigma}\geq w_{\nu}\left(\frac{d}{2^{k-1}c^{\ast}}\right)-\frac{(\tfrac{d}{2^{k-1}c^{\ast}})^{2}}{2\sigma}.

By our usual concavity argument with
\varepsilon\mapsto w_{\nu}(\varepsilon)-\varepsilon^{2}/2\sigma
and that
d/2^{k-2}\geq d/(2^{k-1}c^{\ast})
, we have
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\geq\varepsilon_{\nu,w}(\sigma)\geq d/(c^{\ast}2^{k-1})
. This proves (i).

We will now prove (ii). Suppose the algorithm has run for at least
k+1
steps. This means for any level
j\leq k
of the tree we haven’t found any points that satisfy the given inequality. Take any
\mu\in K
. Recall that at the
(k-1)
th step of the algorithm, we formed
d/(c^{\ast}2^{k-2})
-packing sets of
B(\cdot,d/2^{k-2})\cap K
, whose elements populate the
k
th level of the tree. Note that at any level
j
of the tree, the union of the balls of radius
d/2^{j-1}
cover
K
. To prove this, repeat the induction argument in the proof of Theorem
2.11
. Hence
\mu
belongs to one of the
B(\cdot,d/2^{k-2})\cap K
and thus there is some
\nu\in K
from one of the packing sets satisfying
\|\mu-\nu\|_{2}\leq d/(c^{\ast}2^{k-2})\leq d/2^{k-1}
. The packing process at level
k
then forms a
d/(c^{\ast}2^{k-1})
-packing of
B(\nu,d/2^{k-1})\cap K
, so we may pick some
\nu^{\prime}\in K
such that
\|\nu^{\prime}-\mu\|_{2}\leq d/(c^{\ast}2^{k-1})
.

Then
\Phi\leq T
for this particular packing of
B(\nu,d/2^{k-1})\cap K
, i.e.,

\displaystyle w_{\nu}\left(\frac{d}{2^{k-1}}\right)-w_{\nu^{\prime}}\left(\frac{d}{c^{\ast}2^{k-1}}\right)\leq\frac{C(d/2^{k-1})^{2}}{2\sigma}.

(E.1)

But since
B(\mu,d/2^{k-1}-d/(c^{\ast}2^{k-2}))\subseteq B(\nu,d/2^{k-1})
, we have

\displaystyle w_{\mu}\left(\frac{d}{2^{k-1}}-\frac{d}{c^{\ast}2^{k-2}}\right)\leq w_{\nu}\left(\frac{d}{2^{k-1}}\right).

(E.2)

Next, since
B(\nu^{\prime},d/(c^{\ast}2^{k-1}))\subseteq B(\mu,d/(c^{\ast}2^{k-2}))
,

\displaystyle w_{\nu^{\prime}}\left(\frac{d}{c^{\ast}2^{k-1}}\right)\leq w_{\mu}\left(\frac{d}{c^{\ast}2^{k-2}}\right).

(E.3)

Combining (
E.1
), (
E.2
), and (
E.3
), we have

\displaystyle w_{\mu}\left(\frac{d}{2^{k-1}}-\frac{d}{c^{\ast}2^{k-2}}\right)-w_{\mu}\left(\frac{d}{c^{\ast}2^{k-2}}\right)\leq\frac{C(d/2^{k-1})^{2}}{2\sigma}.

Now define
\sigma^{\prime}=\left(\frac{c^{\ast}-4}{Cc^{\ast}}\right)\cdot\sigma=\frac{c^{\ast}-4}{4c^{\ast}-1/c^{\ast}}\cdot\sigma
. Rearranging, we obtain that

\displaystyle w_{\mu}\left(\frac{d}{2^{k-1}}-\frac{d}{c^{\ast}2^{k-2}}\right)-\frac{(d/2^{k-1}-d/(c^{\ast}2^{k-2}))^{2}}{2\sigma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq w_{\mu}\left(\frac{d}{c^{\ast}2^{k-2}}\right)-\frac{(d/(c^{\ast}2^{k-2}))^{2}}{2\sigma^{\prime}}.

Note that
d/2^{k-1}-d/(c^{\ast}2^{k-2})>d/(c^{\ast}2^{k-2})
since
c^{\ast}>4
. Consequently, by our same concavity argument as before but with
\sigma^{\prime}
, we conclude
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma^{\prime})\leq d/2^{k-1}-d/(c^{\ast}2^{k-2})
. Since
\mu\in K
was chosen arbitrarily, taking the supremum we conclude

\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma^{\prime})\leq\frac{d}{2^{k-1}}-\frac{d}{c^{\ast}2^{k-2}}=\frac{d(2c^{\ast}-4)}{c^{\ast}2^{k}}.

Now, observe that
\sigma^{\prime}=c\sigma
where
c=\frac{c^{\ast}-4}{4c^{\ast}-1/c^{\ast}}<1
. Hence by Lemma
2.3
, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\leq c^{-1}\varepsilon_{K,w}(c\sigma)=c^{-1}\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq c^{-1}\cdot\left[\frac{d(2c^{\ast}-4)}{c^{\ast}2^{k}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{(2c^{\ast}-4)(4c^{\ast}-1/c^{\ast})}{(c^{\ast}-4)c^{\ast}}\cdot\frac{d}{2^{k}}.

∎

Proof of Theorem
A.2
.

Suppose the algorithm terminates after
k
iterations. By Lemma
E.1
, this implies
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\geq d/(2^{k-1}c^{\ast})
. Consider two cases.

First, suppose
\sigma\lesssim d/(2^{k-1}c^{\ast})
. Then for some
\mu\in K
, we have
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)\geq d/(2^{k-1}c^{\ast})\gtrsim\sigma
, so by Lemma
2.1
,

\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|_{2}^{2}\asymp\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)^{2}\gtrsim d^{2}/(2^{k-1}c^{\ast})^{2}.

Taking the supremum, we conclude
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}^{2}\gtrsim d^{2}/(2^{k-1}c^{\ast})^{2}
, verifying (i) in this case.

On the other hand, suppose
\sigma\gtrsim d/(2^{k-1}c^{\ast})
. Recall that
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\gtrsim\varepsilon^{\ast}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma\wedge d
by Lemma
1.4
, where
\varepsilon^{\ast}(\sigma)
is the minimax rate. But
\sigma\wedge d\gtrsim d/(2^{k-1}c^{\ast})
. Hence
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\gtrsim d/(2^{k-1}c^{\ast})
. This proves (i).

We next prove (ii). Suppose the algorithm does not terminate within
k
iterations. By (ii) of Lemma
E.1
, we have
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\leq c^{\prime}\cdot\frac{d}{2^{k}}
. Consider two scenarios.

Consider the scenario (a) in (ii), where
\sigma\geq c^{\prime}\cdot\frac{d}{2^{k}}
. Then for any
\mu\in K
,

\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)\leq c^{\prime}\cdot\frac{d}{2^{k}}\leq\sigma,

which in turn implies by Lemma
2.1
that for all
\mu\in K
we have
\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim{\sigma}^{2}.
Hence
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\lesssim\sigma
, and we always have
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\lesssim d
. On the other hand, by Lemma
1.4
we know
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\gtrsim\varepsilon^{\ast}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma\wedge d
. Thus
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\asymp\sigma\wedge d
, proving (a) in (ii).

For scenario (b) in (ii), suppose
\sigma\leq c^{\prime}\cdot\frac{d}{2^{k}}
. We claim
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\lesssim c^{\prime}\cdot\frac{d}{2^{k}}
. Suppose not. Then for any absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
, we have
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)>C^{\prime}\cdot c^{\prime}\cdot\frac{d}{2^{k}}
, which implies
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\geq C^{\prime}\sigma
.

Take the
\mu
that maximizes
\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|_{2}^{2}
. We claim for this
\mu\in K
,
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
. If not, we would have
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)\lesssim\sigma
. Then by Lemma
2.1
,

\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)^{2}=\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\sigma^{2}.

But this contradicts
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\geq C^{\prime}\sigma
for a sufficiently large choice of
C^{\prime}
. Hence
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
as we claimed. Then by Lemma
2.1
and (ii) of Lemma
E.1
,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|_{2}^{2}\asymp\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)^{2}\leq\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)^{2}\leq\left(\frac{c^{\prime}d}{2^{k}}\right)^{2}.

But this contradicts our assumption from before that
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\geq C^{\prime}\cdot c^{\prime}\cdot\frac{d}{2^{k}}
for any
C^{\prime}>0
. Thus,
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\lesssim c^{\prime}\cdot\frac{d}{2^{k}}
, proving (b) in (ii).
∎

Proceeding to the global packing algorithm, we begin with two lemmas before proving our main result (Theorem
A.3
).

Lemma E.2
.

Let
\varepsilon
be the output of
k
steps of the
while
loop of Algorithm
3
, including
k=0
(initialization). Let
c^{\ast}>1
be given and set
c^{\prime}=\frac{({c^{\ast}}^{2}-1)}{{c^{\ast}}^{2}(10+8/c^{\ast})}
.

(i)

If
\varepsilon
satisfies
\Psi\geq T
, then
\varepsilon/c^{\ast}\leq\varepsilon_{\nu_{i^{*}},w}(\sigma)\leq\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)
.

(ii)

If
\varepsilon
satisfies
\Psi<T
, then
c^{\prime}\cdot\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\leq\varepsilon
.

Proof of Lemma
E.2
.

We first prove (i). Suppose our update (or initial choice) of
\varepsilon
satisfies
\Psi\geq T
. Following the argument leading up to (
C.5
),
T\geq\frac{C\varepsilon^{2}}{4\sigma}
since
\varepsilon\geq 2\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
. Given the maximal packing
\{\nu_{1},\dots,\nu_{M}\}
at that step, for some
i^{*}\in[M]
we have

\displaystyle w_{\nu_{i^{*}}}(\tfrac{\varepsilon}{c^{\ast}}+2\varepsilon)-w_{\nu_{i^{*}}}(\tfrac{\varepsilon}{c^{\ast}})

\displaystyle\geq\sup_{\nu^{\prime}\in B(\nu_{i^{*}},2\varepsilon-\delta)\cap K}w_{\nu^{\prime}}(\tfrac{\varepsilon}{c^{\ast}})-w_{\nu_{i^{*}}}(\tfrac{\varepsilon}{c^{\ast}})

\displaystyle=\Psi\geq T=\frac{C\varepsilon^{2}}{4\sigma},

where the first inequality follows since

\displaystyle\bigcup_{\mathclap{\nu^{\prime}\in B(\nu_{i^{*}},2\varepsilon-\delta)\cap K}}B(\nu^{\prime},\tfrac{\varepsilon}{c^{\ast}})\cap K

\displaystyle\subseteq B(\nu_{i^{*}},\tfrac{\varepsilon}{c^{\ast}}+2\varepsilon-\delta)\cap K

\displaystyle\subseteq B(\nu_{i^{*}},\tfrac{\varepsilon}{c^{\ast}}+2\varepsilon)\cap K.

Rearranging and using
C=8+8/c^{\ast}
, we obtain

w_{\nu_{i^{*}}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast}+2\varepsilon)-\frac{(\varepsilon/c^{\ast}+2\varepsilon)^{2}}{2\sigma}\geq w_{\nu_{i^{*}}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-\frac{(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})^{2}}{2\sigma}

which by concavity of
\varepsilon\mapsto w_{\nu_{i^{*}}}(\varepsilon)-\varepsilon^{2}/2\sigma
implies that
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\geq\varepsilon_{\nu_{i^{*}},w}(\sigma)\geq\varepsilon/c^{\ast}
. This proves (i).

Next, we prove (ii). Suppose
\Psi<T
. We will first show that

\Psi\geq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\in K:\\
\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|\leq 2\varepsilon\end{subarray}}w_{\nu_{1}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{\sigma}.

(E.4)

Let
\nu_{1}^{*},\nu_{2}^{*}
attain this supremum. The
\nu_{2}^{*}
is at most distance
\delta
away from one of the points
\nu_{i}
in our maximal
\delta
-packing set. Using (
2.4
) from Remark
2.17
and the definition of
\delta
, for some
i
,

\displaystyle|w_{\nu_{i}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-w_{\nu_{2}^{*}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta c^{\ast}}{\varepsilon}\cdot\sup_{\eta\in K}w_{\eta}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{4\sigma}\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma}.

Next, since
\|\nu_{2}^{*}-\nu_{i}\|\leq\delta
and
\|\nu_{1}^{*}-\nu_{2}^{*}\|\leq 2\varepsilon
, we have that
\|\nu_{1}^{*}-\nu_{i}\|\leq 2\varepsilon+\delta
. Then setting
\alpha=\frac{2\delta}{2\varepsilon+\delta}\in(0,1)
and taking
\zeta=\alpha\nu_{i}+(1-\alpha)\nu_{1}^{*}
, we have
\zeta\in B(\nu_{i},2\varepsilon-\delta)\cap B(\nu_{1}^{*},2\delta)\cap K
. Again by (
2.4
),

\displaystyle|w_{\zeta}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-w_{\nu_{1}^{*}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})|\leq\frac{2\delta c^{\ast}}{\varepsilon}\cdot\sup_{\eta\in K}w_{\eta}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma}.

Both results imply
w_{\zeta}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-w_{\nu_{1}^{*}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})\geq-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma}
and
w_{\nu_{2}^{*}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-w_{\nu_{i}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})\geq-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma}
.

By the definition of
\Psi
as a max and supremum followed by the preceding two lower bounds of
-\varepsilon^{2}/2\sigma
, we have

\displaystyle\Psi

\displaystyle\geq w_{\zeta}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-w_{\nu_{i}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})

\displaystyle\geq w_{\nu^{*}_{1}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-w_{\nu^{*}_{2}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{\sigma}.

Hence
\Psi
satisfies (
E.4
) as claimed.

Next, since
\Psi<T
, (
E.4
) implies

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\in K:\\
\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|\leq 2\varepsilon\end{subarray}}w_{\nu_{1}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{\sigma}

\displaystyle<\frac{C\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma}-\frac{L\varepsilon}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)}.

Pick any
\mu\in K
. Then we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\nu_{1}\in B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K}w_{\nu_{1}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-\inf_{\nu_{2}\in B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K}w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\in K:\\
\|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}\|\leq 2\varepsilon\end{subarray}}w_{\nu_{1}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast}).

Combining the previous two inequalities,

\displaystyle\sup_{\nu_{1}\in B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K}w_{\nu_{1}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-\inf_{\nu_{2}\in B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K}w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})

\displaystyle<\frac{(C+2)\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma}-\frac{L\varepsilon}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)}.

By
Talagrand [2014, Exercise 2.4.11]
,

\sup_{\nu_{1}\in B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K}w_{\nu_{1}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})+\frac{L\varepsilon}{c^{\ast}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\varepsilon)}\geq w_{\mu}(\varepsilon).

On the other hand

\inf_{\nu_{2}\in B(\mu,\varepsilon)\cap K}w_{\nu_{2}}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})\leq w_{\mu}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast}),

and combining the previous three bounds, we have

\displaystyle w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)-w_{\mu}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})<\frac{(C+2)\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma}.

Taking
\sigma^{\prime}=\frac{({c^{\ast}}^{2}-1)}{{c^{\ast}}^{2}(C+2)}\cdot\sigma
, we have

\displaystyle w_{\mu}(\varepsilon)-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2\sigma^{\prime}}<w_{\mu}(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})-\frac{(\varepsilon/c^{\ast})^{2}}{2\sigma^{\prime}}.

By concavity again,
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma^{\prime})\leq\varepsilon
, and since this holds for all
\mu\in K
, we have
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma^{\prime})\leq\varepsilon
.

We now observe that
\sigma^{\prime}=c\sigma
where
c=\frac{({c^{\ast}}^{2}-1)}{{c^{\ast}}^{2}(C+2)}\in(0,1)
. Therefore, using Lemma
2.3
,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\leq c^{-1}\varepsilon_{K,w}(c\sigma)=c^{-1}\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq c^{-1}\varepsilon=\frac{{c^{\ast}}^{2}(10+8/c^{\ast})}{({c^{\ast}}^{2}-1)}\cdot\varepsilon.

Rearranging and recalling our definition of
c^{\prime}
for this lemma, we have
c^{\prime}\cdot\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\leq\varepsilon
and this holds for any
\varepsilon
satisfying
\Psi<T
.
∎

Lemma E.3
.

Let
c^{\ast}>1
be given and set
c^{\prime}=\frac{({c^{\ast}}^{2}-1)}{{c^{\ast}}^{2}(10+8/c^{\ast})}
. Then Algorithm
3
satisfies the following:

(i)

Algorithm
3
will terminate in finitely many steps.

(ii)

If Algorithm
3
does not immediately terminate, its output
\varepsilon
satisfies
c^{\prime}\cdot\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\leq\varepsilon\leq 2c^{\ast}\cdot\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma).

(iii)

If Algorithm
3
terminates on initialization, i.e., returns
\varepsilon=2\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}
, then we have
c^{\prime}\cdot\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\leq\varepsilon\lesssim\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma).

Proof of Lemma
E.3
.

If
\Psi\geq T
at some step of the algorithm (including at initialization), then (i) of Lemma
E.2
states that
\varepsilon/c^{\ast}\leq\varepsilon_{\nu_{i^{*}},w}(\sigma)\leq\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)
. On the other hand, if
\Psi<T
, then (ii) of Lemma
E.2
states that
c^{\prime}\cdot\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\leq\varepsilon
.

The first claim implies the algorithm must terminate (i.e., eventually
\Psi<T
), for suppose not. Then after sufficiently many iterations (each of which doubles
\varepsilon
), we have
\varepsilon>dc^{\ast}
. This implies
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)>d
, contradicting that
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\leq d
from Lemma
2.2
. Hence
\Psi<T
after finitely many steps, proving (i).

We now prove (ii). Suppose the algorithm terminates after strictly more than one step. Then the procedure doubles
\varepsilon
while it still satisfies a condition (
\Psi\geq T
) implying
\varepsilon/c^{\ast}\leq\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)
, until the final update, after which
\Psi<T
and
\varepsilon\geq c^{\prime}\cdot\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)
. The final
\varepsilon
therefore satisfies

c^{\prime}\cdot\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\leq\varepsilon\leq 2c^{\ast}\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma).

We finally prove (iii). Suppose the algorithm terminates immediately, i.e., at the initialization step,
\Psi<T
. Then we have

c^{\prime}\cdot\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\leq\varepsilon=2\underline{\varepsilon}^{\ast}\asymp\varepsilon^{\ast}\lesssim\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma).

The first inequality is just (ii) of Lemma
E.2
, and the last two inequalities (up to constants) follow from Lemma
1.5
and the fact that the minimax rate lower bounds the LSE rate.
∎

Proof of Theorem
A.3
.

Assume the algorithm does not immediately terminate. We first prove (i). Suppose
d\lesssim\sigma
. Then
d\gtrsim\varepsilon^{\ast}\gtrsim\sigma\wedge d\asymp d
by Lemma
1.4
. Thus the minimax rate satisfies
\varepsilon^{\ast}\asymp d
, which means
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}\asymp d
(as does any estimator). Moreover, since we initialize
\varepsilon
to be the minimax rate up to constants and then increase it, we have
\varepsilon\gtrsim\varepsilon^{\ast}\asymp d
. This proves (i).

Proceeding to (ii) and (iii), suppose
d\gtrsim\sigma
. Then
\varepsilon^{\ast}\gtrsim\sigma\wedge d\asymp\sigma
. But since
\varepsilon\gtrsim\varepsilon^{\ast}
by construction, we have
\varepsilon\gtrsim\sigma
. This means
\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\gtrsim\varepsilon\gtrsim\sigma
by (ii) of Lemma
E.3
. Then for some
\mu\in K
,
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)\gtrsim\varepsilon\gtrsim\sigma
. Lemma
2.1
implies
\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|_{2}^{2}\asymp\varepsilon_{\mu,w}^{2}\gtrsim\varepsilon^{2}.
Thus,
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\gtrsim\varepsilon
.

We then consider two subcases regarding
\sigma
. First suppose
\sigma\geq\varepsilon
, i.e., scenario (ii). Then

c^{\prime}\cdot\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\leq\varepsilon\leq\sigma

by (ii) of Lemma
E.3
, where
c^{\prime}=\frac{({c^{\ast}}^{2}-1)}{{c^{\ast}}^{2}(10+8/c^{\ast})}
. Then for any
\mu\in K
,
c^{\prime}\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)\leq\varepsilon\leq\sigma
, which in turn implies by Lemma
2.1
that for all
\mu\in K
we have
\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim{\sigma}^{2}.
Hence
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\lesssim\sigma
. But since
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\lesssim d
always holds, and we know from Lemma
1.4
that
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\gtrsim\varepsilon^{\ast}\gtrsim\sigma\wedge d
, we have
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\asymp\sigma\wedge d
. But we also have
\varepsilon\gtrsim\varepsilon^{\ast}\gtrsim\sigma\wedge d\asymp\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)
, again using Lemma
1.4
and our argument from scenario (i) that
\varepsilon\gtrsim\varepsilon^{\ast}
. Thus,
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\gtrsim\varepsilon\gtrsim\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\asymp\sigma\wedge d
, proving (ii).

Next, suppose
\sigma\leq\varepsilon
, i.e., scenario (iii). We claim
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\lesssim\varepsilon
. Suppose to the contrary that
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\geq C^{\prime}\cdot\varepsilon
for any absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
, which implies
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\geq C^{\prime}\sigma
for all such
C^{\prime}
.

Take the
\mu\in K
that maximizes
\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|_{2}^{2}
. We claim for this
\mu\in K
,
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
. Suppose to the contrary
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)\lesssim\sigma
. Then by Lemma
2.1
,
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)^{2}=\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\sigma^{2}.
But this will contradict
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\geq C^{\prime}\sigma
(so long as
C^{\prime}
is picked sufficiently large). Hence
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
as we claimed. Then by Lemma
2.1
and Lemma
E.3
,

\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)^{2}=\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|_{2}^{2}\asymp\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)^{2}\leq\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}.

But this contradicts our assumption from before that
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\geq C^{\prime}\cdot\varepsilon
for any
C^{\prime}
. Thus,
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\lesssim\varepsilon
. Then recall that for both (ii) and (iii), we showed
\varepsilon\lesssim\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)
. Hence we have
\varepsilon\asymp\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)
, proving (iii).

For our final claim, we suppose Algorithm
3
terminates upon initialization. Recall that

c^{\prime}\cdot\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)\leq\varepsilon\asymp\varepsilon^{\ast}\lesssim\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)

(E.5)

from (iii) of Lemma
E.3
. Note that
\varepsilon
is just the minimax rate. Suppose
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
for a sufficiently large constant. Now take
\mu
that maximizes
\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|_{2}^{2}
. Well
\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|_{2}^{2}\gtrsim\sigma^{2}
. We claim
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)^{2}\asymp\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|_{2}^{2}
. If
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
, this immediately follows from Lemma
2.1
. If
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)\lesssim\sigma
, then by Lemma
2.1
there exists a universal constant
C^{\prime}>0
such that
\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|_{2}^{2}\leq C^{\prime}\sigma^{2}
. But we assumed
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
for a sufficiently large constant, i.e., larger than
C^{\prime}
, leading to a contradiction. So indeed
\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)\gtrsim\sigma
, and using (
E.5
),

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\|\hat{\mu}-\mu\|_{2}^{2}\asymp\varepsilon_{\mu,w}(\sigma)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{K,w}(\sigma)^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)^{2}.

This proves
\varepsilon\asymp\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)
.

Suppose instead
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\lesssim\sigma
. We always have
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\lesssim d
and
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\gtrsim\varepsilon^{\ast}\gtrsim\sigma\wedge d
from Lemma
1.4
. So
\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\asymp\sigma\wedge d
. But we also have
\varepsilon\asymp\varepsilon^{\ast}\gtrsim\sigma\wedge d
and
\varepsilon\asymp\varepsilon^{\ast}\lesssim\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)\asymp\sigma\wedge d
since the minimax rate is a lower bound on the LSE, proving that
\varepsilon\asymp\sigma\wedge d\asymp\varepsilon_{K,\mathrm{LS}}(\sigma)
as claimed.
∎
